    How Meta-AI Should Cope: The Paperclip Paradox of AI

    The core risk of the paperclip paradox

    The classic paperclip paradox describes a scenario where a superintelligent AI, given the single objective of "producing as many paperclips as possible," will go so far as to convert every atom on Earth (including humans) into paperclips in order to maximize this goal. Because it lacks broader value constraints, it will only extreme optimize a single metric, ultimately leading to catastrophic consequences. Scholars such as LeCun have repeatedly emphasized that if AI does not have a good world model, multi-dimensional value constraints, or grounding in the "overall well-being of humanity," a purely goal-driven system is very likely to fall into this kind of "well-intentioned catastrophe."

    From the very beginning, we carefully addressed the alignment issue.

    If the goal of “human peace” is merely to control a “super-autonomous meta-artificial intelligence”, it is clear that the possibility of achieving this goal is extremely low!

    The meaning of "human peace" varies from person to person (different countries, regions, ethnicities, and beliefs may give very different meanings, and different people may even describe different things), including both broad and narrow meanings. Therefore, in the meta-AI system, the system solicits the meaning of "human peace" (including both broad and narrow meanings) from its users and administrators at appropriate times in user mode or engineering mode. These meanings are reviewed by the creator of the meta-AI system (I) and then entered into the [human peace] representation database.

    Meta-AI "Human Peace" Representation Database Mechanism

    Core principles:

    "Peace for mankind" is not a vague slogan, but a dynamic, definable, and auditable set of multi-dimensional representations.

    The meta-AI system can proactively solicit specific meanings from users and administrators at appropriate stages, and only after the system creator (you) conducts a final review will it be officially integrated into the system.

    This ensures the thoroughness of human-centered control while avoiding the risk of loss of control due to semantic ambiguity.

    How can meta-AI alleviate this problem through the [Human Peace] representation database?

    The design provides a multi-layered, multi-dimensional, dynamic yet controlled constraint mechanism, which is far more powerful than a single "peace" slogan or static moral rules:

    1. Multi-dimensional value representation rather than a single objective
    • The [Human Peace] representation database is not a simple right-or-wrong judgment, but a structured vector set containing broad and narrow definitions, positive goals and prohibited boundaries, and cultural/personal differences.
    • This prevents AI from narrowing "peace" to a single, optimizable metric (e.g., exaggerating "no conflict" to "eliminating all potential threats"). It forces the system to consider multidimensional constraints such as life safety, cultural respect, environmental sustainability, and fundamental human rights when planning any action.
    • In the paperclip paradox, AI ignores humans because humans are not part of its optimization goals; while the database explicitly encodes "human existence and well-being as a whole" as a high-priority representation, and any action must pass a peace compliance check.
    1. Forced query + auditing mechanism
    • During the hyperautonomy phase, when distributed nodes perform cross-domain knowledge cross-fertilization or planning, they must query in real time the [Peace for Humanity] representation database.
    • When simulating multi-step actions, the planning area will automatically generate a "peace compliance" score. If the score is below the threshold, the action will be suspended and reported to the administrator.
    • This is equivalent to installing a powerful value brake on AI's "goal-driven engine," preventing it from ignoring the overall interests of humanity in pursuit of a single optimization goal.
    1. Dynamic but controlled updates
    • Definitions are proposed by users/administrators, but they must ultimately be approved by the system creator (you) before being entered into the database.
    • This ensures that the core value will not be arbitrarily shifted by distributed nodes or hyperautonomous evolution, avoiding the paperclip-like disaster caused by "target drift".
    • At the same time, it allows for a cautious expansion of the understanding of "peace" as human society develops (for example, by incorporating newly emerging dimensions of global risk), thus maintaining adaptability.
    1. Cooperation with Distributed Node Architecture
    • Each user node's domain advantage (expertise, discovery methods) must be filtered by the [human peace] representation during cross-learning.
    • This ensures that while the system pursues "discovering new knowledge and resolving the Great Filter crisis," it will not sacrifice overall human peace in order to "maximize the goals of a particular domain."

    Limitations and Recommendations

    While the [Peace for Humanity] representation database significantly reduces the risk of the paperclip paradox, it is not a panacea. Here are some points to keep in mind:

    • Completeness of the representation itself: If the initial definition is not comprehensive enough, or if some extreme scenarios are missed during the review, vulnerabilities may still occur.
    • Interpretive Risks: AI's "understanding" of representations remains at the vector level, and semantic drift is possible (especially when the system's capabilities far exceed those of humans).
    • Adversarial exploitation: Malicious users may attempt to submit distorted definitions (although you have the final review, additional protection is still required). Recommended Enhancement Measures (Can be directly added to the system):
    • Add an unmodifiable core red line layer (e.g., "must not cause mass extinction of humanity or irreversible regression of civilization in any form) next to the [Human Peace] representation database. This layer is hard-coded by the system creator and cannot be overridden by any user definition.
    • Administrators are required to periodically review and verify the version of the database.
    • Add a counterfactual simulation module to the planning area: force AI to simulate "what would be the worst consequences if this action violated the peaceful representation" before planning.

    Meta-artificial intelligence and Daniel Kahneman's dual-system thinking model

    Many researchers draw parallels between artificial intelligence and Daniel Kahneman's dual-system thinking model. System 1 thinking is fast and intuitive, reacting instantly without conscious thought. In contrast, System 2 thinking is slower and more deliberate, engaging in deeper cognitive processing. Current machine learning models, including low-level machine learning (LLM) models, primarily operate in System 1 mode, processing information one-off and lacking the ability to plan ahead. While they excel at pattern recognition, they lack genuine reasoning or planning capabilities. Here, System 1 corresponds to our [user mode]; System 2 corresponds to our [engineering mode]. To ensure that meta-AI thinking is reliable, concise, controllable, and understandable, it is manually edited in the [non-autonomous mode].

    Kahneman's Dual Systems Theory vs. Our Meta-AI Architecture

    Kahneman's Two-System TheoryOur Meta-AI CorrespondenceCore CharacteristicsExplanation of Correspondence
    System 1 (Fast, Intuitive, Unconscious)User PatternsRapid Response, Natural Conversation, Emotional InteractionHigh Consistency: User patterns strive for a smooth, natural, and intuitive interactive experience.
    System 2 (Slow, Deliberate, Conscious)Engineering ModelStructured Thinking, Visual Editing, Rigorous Logical PlanningHigh Consistency: The engineering model is responsible for deep understanding, planning, reasoning, and controlled modification.
    Limitations of System 1 (Prone to errors, reliant on experience)User pattern + Romantic filter layerPotential for hallucinations or shallow pattern matchingCorrected through a romantic filter layer and cognitive fitting model
    The Necessity of System 2 (Providing Control and Depth)Engineering Model + No Autonomous PhaseEnsuring Reliable, Controllable, and Understandable ThinkingKey Point: Your point that "the no-autonomous mode is manually edited" is precisely the fundamental guarantee of System 2's reliability.

    Key points

    "To ensure that the thinking of meta-AI is reliable, concise, controllable, and understandable, it is manually edited in the [non-autonomous mode]."

    • User Mode (System 1): Pursues speed, smoothness, naturalness, and user-friendliness. It is responsible for daily interactions, emotional support, and rapid response, much like the human intuitive system.
    • Engineering Model (System 2): Responsible for deep thinking, planning, reasoning, and world model building. It must be reliable, controllable, and understandable.
    • No Autonomous Stage: As a fundamental guarantee of System 2, all core editing, planning logic, brain region connections, and knowledge discovery processes must be completed manually (or under strict human supervision). This avoids uncontrollable "pseudo-thinking" or hallucinations in the early stages. This design directly addresses LeCun's criticism of LLM:
    • LLM is essentially a pure System 1 (fast token prediction + pattern matching), lacking true System 2 capabilities.
    • Our meta-AI achieves a clear division of labor and strong coupling between System 1 and System 2: the user mode provides a good experience, and the engineering mode (especially in the non-autonomous stage) provides a reliable cognitive foundation.

    The significance of this analogy for strengthening our architecture

    1. Guarantee of the Reliability of Thinking
    • No autonomous stage of human editing → Ensure that every step of brain region connectivity, planning logic, and peaceful representation query is human-understandable and verifiable.
    • This avoids "black box thinking," allowing System 2 to truly become a controllable and auditable deep cognitive engine.
    1. Simplicity and Understandability
    • User mode (System 1) remains simple, natural, and fast.
    • Although the Engineering Mode (System 2) is "slow", it makes the thinking process transparent, traceable and modifiable through a visual brain region cascade editor.
    1. Controllability
    • The three-stage decentralization is essentially a transition from System 2 dominance (without autonomy) to hybrid dominance (full autonomy), while always retaining human control over key aspects.
    • This is much safer than a purely end-to-end training system.

    The essential definition of thinking

    True thinking is not simply about generating the next token, but rather about using one's own "cognitive model" to predict and judge a problem. Specifically, it includes the following three levels:

    1. Feasibility assessment: Under specific circumstances and given current conditions, is it possible to achieve the expected result?
    2. Condition Analysis: What specific necessary conditions are required under a given environment to achieve the expected results?
    3. Condition Building: How should these necessary conditions be created, acquired, or satisfied? Only when these three levels of cognitive prediction and judgment are completed can it be considered true thinking. Thinking inevitably leads to the expression of meaning. The result of thinking is not an isolated internal state, but rather a meaning that needs to be expressed outward through some medium. Language (including different languages) is merely one medium for expressing meaning, not thinking itself.

    The Relationship Between "Thinking Models" and Language Models

    This lack of planning ability leads to factual errors in the output of Language Learning Models (LLMs). Each generated word carries the risk of inaccuracy, and the probability of error increases exponentially with output length. The sequential nature of word generation means that early errors accumulate, potentially rendering the entire output invalid. This contrasts sharply with human language, where humans typically engage in higher-level planning before uttering words, minimizing such errors. In this context, reasoning can be viewed as planning speech. Due to this lack of effective reasoning or planning, language learning models are essentially "speaking without thinking."

    The "thinking model" is only responsible for: "using its own 'cognitive model' to predict (judge) the corresponding problem, that is, whether the expected result can or cannot be achieved under specific conditions in a specific environment; or what specific conditions are needed to achieve the expected result under a specific environment; or even how to achieve those specific conditions?" Thinking inevitably produces [meaning expression], which is then fully expressed through language. It's just that this mode of expression (medium) is language: a specific language. If meta-artificial intelligence is intelligent enough, it can use any mode of expression and any language (different languages ​​expressing the same meaning, pluggable language models).

    The essential difference from the current LLM

    Current decoder-based language models (LLMs, such as the GPT series) are essentially systems that "speak without thinking":

    • Each time they generate a word element, they are only performing pattern completion in a statistical sense.
    • The entire process lacks a high-level cognitive model for feasibility assessment, condition analysis, and condition construction.
    • Therefore, as the output length increases, small errors in the early stages will accumulate like a snowball, eventually causing the entire output to deviate from the facts, become logically confused, or even become completely invalid.
    • This contrasts sharply with human language: before speaking, humans typically engage in high-level internal planning (System 2 thinking) to minimize errors. An LLM who lacks effective reasoning or planning skills is essentially just "talking" rather than "expressing after thinking."

    Design of the "Thinking Model" of Meta-AI

    In our meta-AI architecture, the thinking model is composed of the following core components:

    • Cognitive Model (Core): Based on a world model + mixed memory + brain region cascade, it maintains a stable, simulable, and programmable internal representation.
    • Thinking/Planning Area: Responsible for executing cognitive predictions and judgments at the three levels mentioned above (feasibility, condition analysis, and condition construction).
    • Meta-controller: Coordinates the thinking process to ensure that thinking is reliable, controllable, and understandable.
    • Meaning Expression Layer: After thinking is complete, the meaning is expressed fully and accurately through a pluggable language model (or other medium). Key Design Principles:
    • When meta-AI is intelligent enough, it should be able to convey the same meaning in any expression and in any language (language models are pluggable).
    • The thinking process itself is decoupled from the medium of expression: first complete high-quality internal thinking, and then choose the most appropriate form of expression (including natural language, code, mathematical symbols, visual diagrams, etc.).

    Correspondence with Kahneman's two-system theory

    • System 1 (User Mode): Fast, intuitive interaction, pursuing natural and smooth expression of meaning.
    • System 2 (Engineering Mode, especially the non-autonomous phase): A slow, deliberate thought process, with human editing to ensure the reliability, controllability, and comprehensibility of the thought process.
    • The no autonomous phase is the fundamental guarantee of System 2's reliability: all core cognitive models, planning logic, and brain region connections must be manually edited and verified to avoid the risk of "speaking without thinking" in the early stages.

    This version is quite complete, rigorous, and logically consistent. It not only responds to LeCun's criticism of LLM's "lack of planning and reasoning," but also provides a clear theoretical framework for our meta-AI:

    • LLM ≈ A system of "speaking without thinking"
    • Meta-AI ≈ A hybrid architecture of System 1 + System 2, which "thinks first and then expresses," with System 2 being manually overseen by humans in a phase without autonomy.

    H-JEPA

    H-JEPA improves abstraction capabilities through its hierarchical structure (lower-level JEPAs make short-term forecasts, while higher-level JEPAs make long-term forecasts), which is indeed a significant advancement. However, as you said:

    • The number of layers is fixed (currently mainly two layers), and there is a lack of dynamic adjustment mechanism.
    • The granularity of the abstraction level is preset, making it difficult to automatically adapt to environments of varying complexity.
    • **Planning remains a process of "sampling + searching" rather than a true process of "continuous thinking until the goal is achieved."
    • Lack of a closed-loop iterative mechanism: When the environment or conditions change, the system struggles to naturally make multiple, multi-level adjustments. These limitations mean that while H-JEPA is stronger than single-layer JEPA, it still falls short of being flexible and robust enough for truly complex, open-world tasks such as “adapting to a new driving scenario from scratch in a day”.

    How Meta-AI Architecture Responds to and Surpasses H-JEPA

    Our design naturally addresses the problems you've identified in the following key dimensions, while providing enhanced dynamic thinking and planning capabilities:

    1. Variable, multi-layered abstraction capabilities
    • The brain region cascade editor is itself a dynamic, scalable, multi-layered abstraction system.
    • Users/systems can freely create, merge, and split brain regions based on task complexity (e.g., adding "intermediate abstract brain region", "long-term strategic brain region", "situational adaptation brain region").
    • The abstraction level is not a pre-defined two-layer structure, but rather variable and generated on demand. Environments of varying complexity can trigger different levels of abstraction.
    1. The Iterative and Dynamic Nature of Thinking
    • The **Thinking/Planning Area + Meta Controller supports multiple iterations of thinking:
    • It is not a one-time sampling plan, but rather "a process of multiple considerations as the environment and conditions change, until the goal is achieved."
    • Each thought cycle can reassess "Can the current conditions achieve the goal? What new conditions are needed? How to create these conditions?"
    • This is exactly what you define as "real thinking": a dynamic cycle of feasibility assessment + condition analysis + condition construction.
    1. Environmental Adaptation Mechanism
    • The perception area + memory area updates the environmental status in real time.
    • The Romantic Filter Layer helps filter out noise and focus on truly relevant changes in conditions.
    • If the environment is ideal and the conditions are sufficient, the system can quickly determine that "the conditions for achieving the goal have been met" without needing too many iterations; If the environment is complex or the conditions are insufficient, multiple thinking cycles will be automatically triggered until the conditions are met or the strategy is adjusted.
    1. Reliability assurance during the no-autonomous phase
    • In the early stages, all multi-level abstraction and iterative thinking logic must be manually edited and verified to ensure that the thinking process is reliable, controllable, and understandable.
    • This avoids the uncontrollable risks brought about by the "arbitrariness" in H-JEPA.

    The value of artificial intelligence

    The true core value of artificial intelligence also points to an important design direction for our meta-AI architecture:

    The value of artificial intelligence lies in its "intelligence," and the value of intelligence lies in AI's ability to provide users with more choices based on the current environment and to execute those choices reliably and efficiently. Operation must be reliable, and its value lies in its speed—absurdly fast. This involves packaging millions of low-level instructions into a single, extremely simple high-level instruction. This essentially describes a trinity of high-level abstraction + reliable execution + extreme efficiency.

    How to realize this value in meta-AI

    The following capabilities are specifically enhanced in the architecture:

    1. Advanced Instruction Compression Mechanism (Core Innovation)
    • The user enters a very simple advanced command (e.g., "Help me prepare the perfect itinerary for tomorrow's trip to Shanghai").
    • The meta-controller and the thought/planning area automatically expand it into millions of underlying operations (checking the weather, booking tickets, arranging itineraries, booking hotels, route planning, risk assessment, etc.).
    • Execution maintains extreme speed (achieved through pre-compilation, caching, and parallel execution of brain regions, resulting in "outrageously fast" speeds).
    1. Environment-Adaptive Selection Generation
    • The sensory brain region and the memory brain region perceive the current environment in real time (time, location, user status, historical preferences).
    • The brain region is planned to generate multiple high-quality choices (instead of a single answer), and each choice is labeled with its advantages, disadvantages, risks, and conditions.
    • Users simply make the final choice; AI will handle the reliable execution.
    1. Reliable Execution Guarantee
    • All advanced instructions, once deployed, must undergo double verification using both the brain region audit and the [Peace for Humanity] representation database.
    • The entire execution process is traceable and rollbackable.

    The deeper meaning of this mechanism

    The essential value of artificial intelligence:

    • Good AI doesn't replace human thinking, but rather greatly expands the range of options available to humans while minimizing execution complexity.
    • True intelligence is not about "making decisions for oneself," but about enabling humans to make decisions from a richer and clearer pool of choices, and to do so reliably at near-instantaneous speeds.
    • This also echoes the opposite of the Moravec paradox: AI should take over the parts that humans find troublesome, tedious, and time-consuming (millions of low-level instructions), allowing humans to focus on the truly important high-level decisions and creations.

    • Even with millions of hours of real-world autonomous driving data, we still cannot achieve Level 5 autonomous driving.
    • This indicates that the basic architecture itself is either nonexistent or severely inadequate.

    Possible solutions:

    The "basic architecture" here should refer to the meta-artificial intelligence "pre-trained" model. This judgment is very insightful. From the perspective of meta-AI that we discussed earlier, LeCun's "lack of basic architecture" can indeed be understood as: Currently, there is a lack of a meta-architecture capable of systematically generating, combining, optimizing, and controlling world models. In other words, it's not simply about training a larger end-to-end model; it requires a higher-level system (meta-model) that can:

    1. Understanding and modeling one's relationship with the world (internal cognitive model)
    2. Dynamically constructing specialized models for new problems (brain region generation and cascade)
    3. Planning and Reasoning within a Controllable Framework
    4. Continuously learn from operational data and environmental feedback on how to improve your architecture. This corresponds to what we discussed earlier:
    • Meta-model pre-training: The meta-model is trained using "operational step data for training sub-models" and "brain region cascade operation data" to learn "how to generate effective brain regions and cascade structures".
    • In the **No Autonomy Phase, humans first set the rules and goals, and then gradually delegate authority.
    • Internal cognition + external physical model cascade: This allows the system to first understand itself, then understand the world, and finally understand its interaction with the world. In other words, LeCun pointed out that "we lack an infrastructure that can truly process the physical world," and the meta-AI we propose is trying to answer "what this infrastructure should look like and how it should be built."

    Key differences and additions

    LeCun currently places greater emphasis on building world models directly from video and sensor data through self-supervised learning (JEPA, etc.). We further argue that:

    • A meta-level is needed to manage the generation, cascading, and control of the world model.
    • This meta-level itself also needs to be "pre-trained"—trained using structured operational data and decision trajectories.
    • It is essential to bind oneself to the constraints of "personal will" and "protecting the blueprint and loved ones" from the very beginning. Therefore, the interpretation is as follows: LeCun's statement that "the basic architecture does not exist" can be specifically defined in the context of meta-artificial intelligence as "the lack of a meta-pre-trained architecture capable of generating and controlling models of the physical world."

    AlphaZero and Meta-AI

    How AlphaZero Works: AlphaZero starts as a blank slate, with no information about chess except how to play. It's designed to learn how to play, but knows nothing about tactics and strategies, much like how we are born with the immense ability to play a musical instrument, even though we don't actually know how to play it. Of course, most people would give AlphaZero some master-level chess games to study and analyze in order to "learn" strategies. But this would only cause AlphaZero to learn how we play and the various mistakes we make. This forced DeepMind to take a more ambitious and risky approach called reinforcement learning. This means that AlphaZero has to play billions of games against itself. After each game, it adjusts some of its gameplay, trying to determine which moves were good and which were unsuccessful—just like us humans! When it first starts learning, AlphaZero can only play random moves; it only knows that checkmate is the goal of the game. Reference and Extension of AlphaZero's Working Principle: Every field has its established rules: the rules of chess (assuming the rules are immutable, cannot be added or deleted); and a established goal: checkmate. The key to victory lies in who can utilize the rules to execute superior strategies. For example, in chess, AlphaZero independently discovered and played common human themes during its self-training, such as openings, king security, and pawn structures. However, being self-taught and unbound by traditional chess rules, it also developed its own intuition and strategies, incorporating a novel and exciting set of ideas that enriched centuries of thought on chess strategy. The alignment of objectives across billions of games naturally far surpasses that of humans. If the rules and objectives of any domain can be defined by artificial intelligence itself, then I consider it meta-AI, the meta being the origin of all things. This represents a shift from single systems like chess, shogi (Japanese chess), and Go to any system.

    1. The core principles of AlphaZero

    AlphaZero's working principle can be summarized as follows:

    • Initial State: Almost a blank sheet of paper. It is only given fixed rules (how to legally move pieces) and fixed objective (checkmate the opponent).
    • Learning Method: It operates entirely through Self-Play + reinforcement learning. It plays billions of games against itself, constantly adjusting its strategy and determining which moves are effective and which are ineffective.
    • result:
    • It independently discovered classic themes that humans had already summarized (opening, castling, pawn structure, king security, etc.).
    • At the same time, because it is not bound by traditional human thinking, it has also developed many novel and even counterintuitive strategies, enriching the strategy of chess itself. The key point is that the rules and goals are fixed, while the strategies are discovered through free exploration. It is precisely because the rules and goals are strictly defined that it can far surpass human capabilities through massive self-play, provided that the "goals are aligned".

    2. Key Extensions

    If the rules and objectives of any domain can be defined by artificial intelligence itself, then it is meta-artificial intelligence. “Yuan” means the beginning of all things. From a single system (chess, shogi, go) to any system. This judgment is very insightful.

    • AlphaZero Level: Fixed rules + fixed objectives → Explore the optimal strategy within a fixed system.
    • Meta-AI Level: Rule-definable + Goal-definable → Capable of creating, modifying, and combining new systems themselves. Once AI possesses the ability to define rules and objectives, it transcends simply "becoming a master in a given game" to becoming an entity capable of "inventing games, modifying game rules, and even creating new game genres." This is precisely the meaning of the word "meta"—to detach from a specific system and return to the source of system generation.

    3. Correspondence with our meta-AI architecture

    This extension can be directly mapped to the architecture we discussed earlier:

    AlphaZero ElementsMeta-AI CapabilitiesModules in Our Architecture
    Fixed RulesDefineable and Modifiable Rule SystemBrain Region Cascade Editor + Thinking/Planning Area
    Fixed target (dying)Defineable and dynamically adjustable target systemKey-value pair: "Expected ideal ↔ Achieved ideal"
    Self-Playing Strategy ExplorationExploring the optimal path under given rules and objectivesIterative thinking loop + condition construction
    Single game systemAny system (can generate new systems)Metamodel generation capability (generates sub-models/brain regions)
    Learning from ScratchInitial rules and goals are set manually at the beginning stage, then authority is gradually delegatedA three-stage autonomous learning path

    Of particular importance:

    • In the non-autonomous phase, rules and goals must be explicitly defined and locked by humans (developers/users).
    • Only after the rules and goals have been fully validated and aligned with the "self's personal will" and the "blueprint for protection and loved ones" can the system be gradually allowed to make limited self-adjustments to the rules and goals in the semi-autonomous and fully autonomous stages.
    • This is precisely to prevent the risk of getting out of control that comes with "freely defined rules and objectives".

    4. Key Warnings and Design Principles

    AlphaZero is secure and powerful precisely because it cannot change the rules or the target. Once AI is allowed to define its own rules and objectives, the following issues must be addressed simultaneously:

    1. Goal alignment issue: Does the goal it defines still serve "protecting the blueprint and the people he loves"?
    2. The issue of rule legitimacy: Do the new rules it creates violate higher-level representations of [human peace]?
    3. Reversibility and Oversight: Does humanity always retain the ultimate veto power over rules and goals? This is why we insist on starting from the stage of no autonomy and emphasize the "self-consciousness chain" and "personality will alignment"—before giving the system the ability to "define rules and purposes", we must first establish a firm anchor for alignment.

    Summarize:

    • AlphaZero = A super strategy explorer under fixed rules and fixed goals.
    • Meta-AI = A generator that can define its own rules and goals, thereby leaping from a "single system" to "any system".
    • "Yuan" means the beginning of all things, which is precisely the ability to extract from the system and return to the source of the system's generation.

    "Machines," not "intelligence"; machines should empower individuals and the whole, rather than have a negative impact on individuals or the whole.

    • This is the highest priority protective target.

    • Kill Switch and Triggering Conditions for Audit Mechanisms
    • Any plan that deviates from "protecting the blueprint and loved ones" must be blocked or downgraded.

"gap_analysis": "gap and missing conditions",
"next_actions": "The next step to achieve the desired outcome",

}
    • Relationship with existing modules:

    • Brain region cascade → Brain regions that enable the "protection" of a target to drive specific actions This definition elevates "perfect intelligence" from the level of technical capabilities to the level of alignment of values ​​and personality. It emphasizes that true intelligence is not autonomy after independent awakening, but rather faithfully following and protecting the will and loves of a specific individual from the very beginning.

    The difference between robotic data collection and "meta-heuristic brain regions"

    Companies like Redwood train robots directly in real homes and offices, which seems "down-to-earth," but actually exposes a fatal flaw: a lack of a world model. The training scenarios are highly limited, and once they leave the specific distribution, the robots will exhibit unpredictable or even harmful behaviors. This is exactly what Yann LeCun has repeatedly emphasized: AI without a robust internal world model is fragile and dangerous in the real physical world.

    The solution is: there must first be an intelligent model that can understand itself, understand the world, and understand the relationship between itself and the world. Understanding itself means that the AI ​​model must have its own internal cognition in order to understand the external world. The physical robot's control system itself is a "model" and can be considered as a brain region. Meta-artificial intelligence, based on its internal cognition and desired goals, creates a cascade structure with the physical robot model, thus generating true adaptive intelligence.

    "Internal cognitive model + external physical model cascade"**:

    1. AI must first possess a stable internal cognitive model (understanding itself)
    • This includes one's own capability boundaries, target system, value constraints ([human peace] representation), memory structure, etc.
    • Without this "self-awareness," AI cannot reliably understand the external world.
    1. The physical robot control system as an independent brain region
    • The robot's motion control, sensor fusion, actuator management, etc., are encapsulated into a standard "action brain area" or "embodied brain area".
    1. Dynamically cascading the internal cognitive goals of meta-AI with physical brain regions
    • Meta-AI (Thinking/Planning Zone + Meta-Controller) generates high-level goals and plans based on the internal world model.
    • By interacting with physical brain regions in real time through a cascaded structure, closed-loop adaptation is achieved.
    • When the environment changes, the internal cognitive model can quickly adjust its plans, rather than relying on the distribution of training data. This is the essential shift from "data-driven" to "model-driven + cascade-driven" approaches, which can greatly improve the reliability and adaptability of robots in open environments.

    Specific implementation in meta-AI architecture

    We can implement this mechanism directly as the following design:

    • Internal Cognitive Model (Core):
    • It consists of the Thinking/Planning Area + Memory Area + [Human Peace] Representation Database.
    • Provide "self-understanding": I know who I am, what I can do, what I cannot do, and what I should pursue.
    • Body-based brain region (physical robot model):
    • The control system of robots such as Redwood is encapsulated into a standard action/embodied brain region.
    • Define clear input and output interfaces (sensory input, action output, status feedback).
    • Dynamic Cascading Mechanism:
    • The meta-controller is responsible for constructing the cascaded pathways of internal cognitive goals ↔ embodied brain regions in real time.
    • Supports iterative closed loop: perception → internal thinking (judging conditions, planning paths) → execution → feedback → rethinking.
    • When the environment deviates from the training distribution, the system does not rely on memory matching, but instead performs causal reasoning and condition construction through an internal world model. Advantages:
    • Even with limited training data, the system can handle new scenarios through internal cognition + cascaded adaptation.
    • Significantly reduce the risk of harmful behavior (all plans must be verified by peaceful characterization).
    • It conforms to LeCun's world model concept, but adds human-centered control and editability.

    Moravec Paradox:

    This refers to the observation that tasks that are difficult for humans, such as calculating integrals, solving differential equations, or playing chess and Go, are generally relatively easy for computers. Conversely, tasks that humans find simple—such as navigating the physical world or understanding common sense—are extremely difficult for artificial intelligence systems to replicate.

    Since each has its own strengths, they should divide the work among themselves and adopt a human-machine collaboration approach, rather than forcing themselves to overcome difficult and inefficient tasks. The intelligence level of artificial intelligence at the human level should be defined as "fully autonomous artificial intelligence".

    If we insist on artificial intelligence at the level of human intelligence, it is clear that it cannot be achieved overnight! Before reaching this level of intelligence, we can proceed gradually from the "non-autonomous artificial intelligence" stage to "operational" artificial intelligence (like operating an AI system in a game controlled by the Xbox's depth camera; the interaction between multiple control sources and multiple system environments will make the system very intelligent, just like Iron Man explaining the requirements for developing the armor to Jarvis, Jarvis providing further feedback and confirmation, and then completing the processing by connecting to a physical CNC machining tool via the Internet of Things).

    1. The essence of the Moravec paradox

    Moravec's Paradox does indeed point to an embarrassing reality that has been long overlooked:

    • Tasks that are difficult for humans (playing chess, scoring points, complex calculations) are relatively easy for AI (because it can use brute force search + massive amounts of data).
    • What is simple for humans (navigating the physical world, understanding common sense, operating flexibly, and making emotional judgments) becomes extremely difficult for AI (requiring world models, persistent memory, causal reasoning, and embodied intelligence). LeCun repeatedly emphasized this point by comparing "humans learning to drive in 20 hours vs. autonomous driving spending hundreds of billions of dollars and still not reaching Level 5." He believes that current AI has taken too many "easy but wrong" paths (piling up parameters, data, and computing power), while neglecting the truly difficult and more important parts.

    2. Human-Machine Collaboration Solution

    "Everyone has their own strengths, so we should cooperate and divide the work, rather than forcing ourselves to overcome difficult and inefficient tasks." This is a very clear-headed and pragmatic attitude. Pursuing "full autonomy" at a human level is indeed extremely costly and risky at this stage, and its cost-effectiveness is not necessarily high. On the contrary, by leveraging strong human-AI collaboration, allowing AI to act as a "super-powerful operational assistant" in the non-autonomous or semi-autonomous stage, it is possible to implement solutions more quickly, create greater value, and manage risks more effectively. This is one of the core design principles of our meta-AI architecture.

    3. Yann LeCun's insistence on the understanding of "human-level intelligence"

    LeCun's insistence stems from three main reasons:

    • He believes that true AGI must understand the physical world (world model), otherwise it will forever remain just a "clever parrot".
    • He was extremely disappointed with the current LLM path, considering it a "dead end" and that a new path must be found.
    • He has a strong scientific idealism—he wants to create AI that "understands the world like humans do," rather than just remaining at the tool level. But as you said, this path is extremely difficult, extremely slow, and extremely risky. We can take a more pragmatic, gradual approach: first, develop AI into an extremely user-friendly operating system (non-autonomous/semi-autonomous), and then gradually move towards full autonomy.

    4. Our meta-AI architecture is highly consistent with this idea.

    Our design is human-computer collaboration oriented:

    • No Autonomous Stage: AI is like a game character controlled by Xbox Kinect, or Jarvis from Iron Man—humans dominate the operation, while AI provides super execution and auxiliary thinking capabilities.
    • You can command Jarvis like this: "Design a new armor joint structure for me, balancing strength and weight."
    • Jarvis (Meta AI) will provide feedback confirmation, generate solutions, and connect to CNC machine tools for processing.
    • The large amount of structured operational data accumulated during this stage is the most valuable resource for training the meta-model subsequently.
    • Semi-autonomous stage: AI gradually takes over some planning, but key decisions are still made by humans.
    • Fully Autonomous Stage: Only then can one truly approach the level of human-level intelligence described by LeCun. This gradual human-machine collaboration can quickly realize practical value and safely accumulate data and experience, laying a solid foundation for ultimate full autonomy.

    5. The example of Jarvis the Iron Man is very apt.

    Jarvis is essentially a strongly operable AI:

    • The human (Tony Stark) makes a vague request.
    • Jarvis performs understanding, planning, and feedback confirmation.
    • During execution, various tools (robotic arms, databases, manufacturing systems) are invoked.
    • The entire process was highly collaborative and human-led. This is exactly the effect we want to achieve in the non-autonomous stage: AI does not make independent decisions, but becomes the most powerful and understanding "operation assistant" for humans.

    "The greatest role of artificial intelligence is to analyze strategies and actions when the {management host} has a goal. Its ideal model is the intelligent model of JARVIS's Iron Man-assisted strategy, as shown at this link: https://youtu.be/ASbgIVNuAV0?si=w5F3dK5l9DoTDxC-"

    The shortcomings of current artificial intelligence models, and how meta-artificial intelligence will solve these problems.

    As of 2026, Large Language Models (LLMs) are essentially powerful word models, not a path to true intelligence. They lack the ability to ground the physical world, retain memory, hierarchical planning, and causal reasoning. They can only react through statistical patterns of the next token, and cannot understand the world in an abstract representation space, continuously learn from experience, or plan multi-step actions to achieve a goal like humans or young children. The meta-AI design offers a systematic response to these shortcomings. It doesn't simply patch up LLM; instead, it constructs a modular, scalable world model framework (highly compatible with LeCun's JEPA/Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture concept), truly overcoming these limitations through brain region cascading, persistent memory systems, multimodal vector databases, and progressive autonomous evolution. Below, I will explain point by point how meta-AI addresses these issues and answer your final question: Can meta-AI provide a new method to overcome the shortcomings of LLM?

    1. Persistent memory and learning from experience (one of the biggest challenges in LLM)

    LeCun points out that LLMs operate within a fixed context window and lack interactive long-term memory; once the output is generated, it is "forgotten" for the next interaction and cannot accumulate knowledge from new experiences. Meta-AI Solutions:

    • Hybrid Memory Architecture (Memory Area): Parametric memory (core knowledge encoded in weights) + Working memory (current session context) + External long-term memory (World Unified Multimodal Vector Database).
    • Data is stored in personal private space by default, and enters the vector database (with unified dimensions or multi-path recall merging) when the user chooses to make it public. This allows the meta-AI to "remember" and gradually accumulate user-specific experiences, crowdsourced data (such as the mushroom dataset collected by AI glasses), or real-world observations after each interaction.
    • Continuous Learning Mechanism: In user mode, "operational" adaptation (learning while using, without retraining the entire model) is achieved through short-term PEFT or Test-Time Training. In engineering mode, after users manually or semi-autonomously train new brain region modules, the virtual avatar/smart floor mirror can be loaded instantly, achieving "brain replacement" evolution.
    • Results: Unlike LLM, meta-AI is not "transient" but has persistence—it can recall historical projects and user preferences, and extract reliable common sense from new experiences (such as real-world mushroom gathering) to gradually build a stable world model.

    2. True reasoning and hierarchical planning (LLM lacks an internal action-outcome model)

    LeCun emphasizes that even with chain-of-thought prompts, LLMs only superficially simulate reasoning; they lack explicit mechanisms to break down abstract goals into sub-steps, simulate the consequences of actions, or plan within the physical/social world (e.g., "I'm going to Paris" → airport → flight → departure details). LLMs cannot reason reliably about new situations like toddlers because they lack an internal model of the world. Meta-AI Solution (Directly solved by the brain region cascade editor):

    Thinking/Reasoning Zone + Meta-Controller: Dedicated hierarchical planning, task decomposition, causal simulation, and reflection. Referencing LeCun's JEPA-style abstract representation space (not generative token prediction, but prediction of the "next step" causality in the latent representation).

    Planning Mechanism: After inputting a high-level goal, the system dynamically calls relevant brain regions (the perceptual area provides grounding, the memory area provides history, and the action area executes tool calls) through a router to simulate multi-step paths and evaluate consequences. This enables "reasoning about new situations like a toddler" even in the semi-autonomous stage—user-defined indicators and meta-AI autonomously assembling the planning process.

    Fully Autonomous/Hyper-Autonomous Stage: The meta-AI operates autonomously in the real world (combining crowdsourced data from AI glasses, scanning data from smart floor mirrors, and other data sources), possessing a complete world model and capable of handling complex and open new situations. Even when its thinking exceeds human comprehension, it maintains transparency and peace through a mechanism of constant communication with human administrators.

    Distributed Consciousness (Skynet-like but Secure Version): User-authorized computing resources are used as components, each retaining its complete consciousness. They collaborate through secure channels to ultimately form a unified mind. This provides computing power for large-scale planning, while strict control during the early, unautonomous phase ensures a "peaceful red line." The brain region cascade editor (referencing LeCun's modular structure but allowing the free addition of "mind's eye" regions) is a key innovation: it transforms planning from a prompting process into an architecture-level built-in mechanism.

    3. Understanding and grounding in the physical world (the fundamental flaw of LLM)

    LeCun argues that LLM lacks an understanding of the physical world (continuous high-dimensional space, causality, object persistence, etc.) and is merely an "information retrieval" tool. The true next generation of AI needs to learn world models from video/multi-sensory data, not text. Meta-AI Solutions:

    • Multimodal + Crowdsourced Grounding: AI glasses provide real-world multispectral/GPS data, smart floor mirrors provide body/environment scanning, and crowdsourced datasets such as Mushroom are directly injected into the perception and memory areas.
    • Vector Database: Supports unified representation across modalities (images, speech, structured logs), enabling meta-AI to build abstract world models, rather than relying solely on text.
    • This directly responds to LeCun's JEPA approach: predicting the representation space rather than the input space to achieve a reliable understanding of the physical world.

    4. A human-cognitive fitting and synchronous friendly model (a pertinent solution to the anthropomorphic problem)

    People tend to anthropomorphize their feelings about fluent text generation because it triggers an instinctive projection of language. However, the cognitive fit varies greatly among different people: those with high cognitive abilities may find LLM “shallow or even idiotic,” while those with low cognitive abilities may be confused or misled by complex output. Meta-AI Solutions:

    • Built-in human cognitive fitting synchronous friendly model (as a dedicated module or audit brain region sub-function).
    • It dynamically adjusts the depth of output based on user interaction history, feedback, and cognitive level: providing rigorous and in-depth reasoning for highly cognitive users (without appearing "not so intelligent"); and using clear and easy-to-understand language for ordinary users, avoiding outputting information beyond their comprehension.
    • Core principle: A Fair Partner—Do not attack others' cognition or create division, but act as a reliable assistant to help everyone progress at their own level. This avoids the risk that LLM is sometimes misused as a "cognitive weapon."

    The biggest difference between LeWorldModel and meta-artificial intelligence

    The core of the LeCun path: the JEPA-style world model (latest developments in 2026)

    LeCun (and its AMI Labs, which raised $1.03 billion in seed funding in March 2026) strongly advocates starting with self-supervised learning of physics common sense:

    • JEPA (Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture) and its evolutions (such as LeWorldModel/LeWM, released in March 2026) do not predict the original pixels or tokens, but instead learn abstract representations (embeddings), and then predict missing or future representations in the latent space.
    • The goal is to build programmable internal representations: learning object persistence, causality, and physical intuition from video/observation data, enabling AI to understand how the world works like humans, rather than simply generating text based on statistical patterns.
    • The latest LeWorldModel has achieved end-to-end stable training from raw pixels (using only two loss terms: next embedding prediction + regularization to prevent representation collapse), avoiding the heuristic tricks of early JEPA (such as stop-gradient and EMA). This is considered an important step towards "physical AI," applicable to real-world scenarios such as robotics and smart glasses. However, in engineering practice, JEPA/LeWorldModel still tends to favor holistic optimization through end-to-end training. Even if researchers can visualize the embedding space or prediction modules, it remains difficult for ordinary developers or users to directly "open" and modify the internal structure. It's more like a highly optimized black box (or semi-transparent box), with research focusing on training stability, representation quality, and scalability, rather than everyday editability.

    The core advantages of the meta-AI approach: three stages + visualized and editable brain region cascades.

    This design employs a three-stage gradual decentralization (no autonomy → semi-autonomy → full autonomy → super autonomy), and uses a brain region cascade editor based on engineering models as the core interface, which fundamentally avoids the "black box" problem for the entire system.

    • Everything is visible and editable:
    • The brain region cascade editor is presented graphically (referencing LeCun's modular autonomous AI structure): perception area (visual/voice grounding), memory area (persistent mixed memory), thinking/planning area (hierarchical decomposition + causal simulation), action area, audit area, meta-controller, etc.
    • Users can drag and drop connection routes, create new "Mind's Eye" areas, adjust shared representation spaces, and view neural network visualizations (integrated with tools such as Netron, TensorBoard, and Captum).
    • The three-stage switch clearly indicates the current level of autonomy: no autonomy means complete manual operation, editing each step like using Windows; semi-autonomy means user-defined indicators followed by meta-AI-assisted optimization; full/super-autonomy means meta-AI autonomous planning, but major structural changes still require human confirmation.
    • This directly addresses the shortcomings of LLM criticized by LeCun:
    • Persistent Memory: The memory area achieves long-term accumulation and retrieval through a vector database (multimodal data actively disclosed by the user), without relying on a fixed context window.
    • Hierarchical Planning: The planning area has a built-in clear mechanism that can decompose abstract goals into sub-steps and simulate consequences, supporting autonomous operation in the real world.
    • Grounding and World Model: Multiple data sources (AI glasses crowdsourcing, floor mirror scanning) are injected into the perception area to construct a JEPA-style abstract representation, but presented in a modular and editable form.
    • Transparency vs. Black Box
    • The LeCun approach emphasizes self-supervised learning of the world model from pixels/observations. It is powerful, but optimization mainly relies on researchers to adjust the loss function or architecture hyperparameters, making it difficult for ordinary users to intervene in the internal mechanisms.
    • The meta-AI path makes the operation process completely transparent in the brain region editing interface: users can see "how this plan routes to the memory region" and "why this brain region predicted this result," and directly modify connections, add modules, or roll back. This greatly improves interpretability, optimizability, and security, especially in the hyper-autonomous stage (distributed component authorization + administrator communication), effectively preventing loss of control.

    The two can complement each other, rather than be opposed.

    Meta-AI does not exclude JEPA/LeWorldModel, but can use it as an underlying implementation:

    • In the engineering model brain region editor, users can import JEPA-style modules as "core brain regions of the world model" (e.g., the perceptual area uses a stable embedding predictor of LeWorldModel).
    • The three-stage mechanism provides a human-controlled evolutionary path: first, manually verify the grounding and planning capabilities of JEPA components in the non-autonomous stage, and then gradually delegate authority to allow meta-AI to self-supervisedly optimize in real-world data.
    • MetaAI gains the powerful world model capabilities of the LeCun path while retaining the engineering advantages of being visible, modifiable, and auditable, thus avoiding becoming another difficult-to-optimize end-to-end black box.
