    Artificial intelligence: "Friday" takes action by analyzing Captain America's attack patterns.

    Meta-level - Definition of Artificial Intelligence (Architecture):

    When faced with unprecedented problems, it can generate a "solution model" for that problem. That is, AI (solution model) is generated in real time by AI (meta-level).

    This artificial intelligence model architecture ultimately generates a model that can effectively solve such problems, which can then be saved and called upon whenever needed.

    This kind of artificial intelligence architecture, which has the ability to generate artificial intelligence model programs, can be called a meta-level artificial intelligence architecture (hereinafter referred to as: meta-artificial intelligence).

    Contemporary Grand ModelMeta-Artificial Intelligence
    ObjectiveTo generate content (tokens, code, images, etc.) on the fly within the pre-trained parameter spaceTo achieve autonomy level differentiation and generate new models (architecture, algorithm, training scheme) on the fly within the meta-level AI architecture. Gradually, the optimal outcome is: Highly beneficial × Hyperautonomous × Overfitting × Beneficial and controllable = 1
    Relies onExtensive pre-prepared data and architectural templates by humansThe goal is to enable the autonomous design of data organization methods, loss functions, and even new connection paradigms for previously unseen problem types.
    OutputOutput can be a piece of text, a piece of code, or an image.Output can be a deployable, fine-tunable, and continuously optimized AI system.
    CapabilitiesLacking basic general capabilities for independently modifying the underlying architectureThe goal is to achieve a closed-loop capability of "model-to-model generation"
    Despite the existence of products on the market that claim to be recursive self-improvement, i.e., the goal is for the model to improve itself (including at the architectural level), we have not seen any effective "products" to date (October 21, 2026).

    Current market situation (October 30, 2026):

    • Extreme automation of Neural Architecture Search (NAS)
      It has moved from "helping humans find better Transformer variants" to "inventing architectures with almost no human template constraints". Some systems can even be inspired by mathematical principles or physical oscillators to independently design new paradigms such as state-space models and linear oscillator networks.
    • The full extension of AutoML (not just search architecture)
      Modern AutoML pipelines can already:
      • Automatically construct dataset augmentation/synthesis strategies
      • Automatically selects/blends multiple algorithms (tree model + neural network + symbolic regression, etc.)
      • Automatic search loss function, optimizer combination
      • Some systems are beginning to experiment with a joint search architecture + data processing + training hyperparameters.
    • The AI ​​Scientist-type system (closest to your description of "generating solution models for unprecedented problems")
      Sakana AI's The AI ​​Scientist has already been able to:
      • Given a research direction/initial codebase
      • Automatically read papers → generate new ideas → write experimental code → run experiments → draw graphs → write a complete paper
    • Recursive Self-Improvement
      In 2025, Meta publicly stated that it was working on self-improving AI, with the goal of enabling models to improve themselves (including at the architectural level).
    The creation of a successful "product" is inevitably accompanied by problems that need to be solved, and these problems are often not solvable overnight.

    for example:

    • Computational cost: Without any constraints, allowing the "model" to explore on its own, training a state-of-the-art (SOTA) model requires 10²⁵–10²⁶ FLOPs, and searching for a new architecture is typically 10–1000 times more expensive. The cost of "building a model on-site" each time is even higher, unless there is a fundamental paradigm breakthrough (such as predictive zero-shot NAS, one-time joint search, etc.).
    • Evaluation difficulties: New problems often lack readily available benchmarks, so how do we know if the generated model is "good" or not? Meta-AI needs to define its own evaluation metrics, which is itself an even more difficult meta-problem.
    • The limit of generalization: All current systems perform well in domains where they have "seen many similar tasks," but they are far from being able to reliably design for domains that "humans have never solved before" (new physics, new branches of mathematics, extremely sparse data tasks).
    • Closed-loop stability: If you allow a model to generate another model, and then allow the new model to generate an updated model, it is easy for degradation, pattern collapse, or exponential resource explosion to occur.
    How should we solve these problems?

    Computational cost issue: Strategically allocate local and cloud computing resources with the goal of "making AI a better assistant rather than replacing one's own thinking and judgment".

    The difficulty of assessment lies in the fact that assessments are conducted by humans (developers) who define the assessment metrics themselves. Only when artificial intelligence reaches a sufficiently "intelligent level" can it define its own assessment metrics.

    The problem of generalization limits: Current artificial intelligence is trained on data, and generative AI is full of randomness. Therefore, the intelligence of artificial intelligence should be reflected in "operation." Sometimes people do it intuitively (mostly after hundreds or even more failures, like Edison inventing the light bulb?). So before artificial intelligence can truly possess the intelligence to "generate solution models for unprecedented problems" on its own, it needs to be controlled by humans (developers) like controlling a Windows operating system. This involves integrating all the necessary functions into the system to handle data processing, algorithm and model architecture selection, deployment, testing, and going live. This helps meta-AI observe useful and reliable patterns.

    Closed-loop stability issues: Generally, it is not allowed to allow new models to generate newer models. As mentioned above, "before artificial intelligence can truly possess the intelligence to generate solution models for unprecedented problems on its own, it needs to be controlled by humans (developers)" to effectively avoid degradation, pattern collapse, or exponential resource explosion problems.

    Inspired by "meta-philosophies" such as the I Ching: Unlike the "flat" recursive self-improvement products on the market, we chose a "dynamic and three-dimensional" meta-architecture to achieve a dynamic balance of target adaptation.
    A fully autonomous, highly advanced, and beneficial intelligence will inevitably emerge, but it seems unlikely to be achieved overnight. Its emergence will more likely involve a long growth process: a three-stage evolution (no autonomy → semi-autonomy → full autonomy). A dynamic and three-dimensional meta-architecture would be a good "cradle" for this.

    How will we build this cradle?

    A preview of the overall system's current integrity:

    The following is a complete summary of the basic architecture of Meta-AI. This architecture is centered on human-centeredness and adopts a modular, visual, and controllable evolution design, clearly distinguishing the capabilities and boundaries of different stages, and striving to achieve a balance between powerful capabilities and secure control.

    I. Overall Design Concept

    • Human-centric control: AI is always an assistant and amplifier to humans, with the ultimate value judgment and key review authority remaining in the hands of humans (especially the system creators).
    • Transparent and Editable: Avoiding the black box, all core mechanisms (brain regions, planning, memory, filtering) are visible and modifiable in engineering mode.
    • Gradual Evolution: Through a strict three-stage (or four-stage) empowerment path, gradually moving from tools to symbiotic intelligence.
    • Dual-engine approach: Culture-driven ([Human Peace] representation database) + Value-driven (token incentives).
    • Responsible Design: Built-in abuse prevention, attention protection, and ethical review mechanisms.

    II. Core Architecture Components

    1. Dual-mode interface system

    • User Mode (for regular users)
    • Virtual avatar + Step-Audio 2 / Step-Audio 2 mini (end-to-end voice interaction)
    • Smart dressing mirror (physical hardware, providing real-time clothing recommendations)
    • Multi-source combined control for VR/AR glasses (gesture + voice + eye tracking)
    • Romantic Filter Layer: Precisely filters "information that belongs to you" from a massive amount of data, adjusting output temperature and depth.
    • Human cognitive fitting synchronous friendly model: dynamically adjusts the output according to the user's cognitive level (avoiding overfitting or information overload).
    • Engineering Mode (for developers/engineers)
    • Brain region cascade editor (core visual interface, allowing drag-and-drop creation/modification of brain regions, routes, and connections)
    • OS-style vector database operation panel
    • Integration of full-visualization tools for neural networks (Netron, TensorBoard, Captum, etc.)
    • Coding Assistant (Target → Strategy → Tool Alignment, Supports Graphical Programming + OS Integration)
    • Knowledge discovery and publishing module (paper-like mechanism)

    2. Three-Stage (Four-Stage) Self-Directed Progression Path

    • No Autonomous Stage (Default Main Force): Completely human-controlled, like operating Windows, manually editing brain regions, training models, and publishing resources.
    • Semi-autonomous stage: Users define metrics, and AI assists in completing some tasks, but key actions require human review.
    • Fully Autonomous Stage: AI possesses its own ethical indicators (based on the [human peace] metric) and can plan and act autonomously, but major changes still require review.
    • Super-autonomous stage: Possesses its own mind and distributed consciousness, capable of thinking in ways that humans may find difficult to understand, but always maintains communication with human administrators and is fundamentally bound by "human peace".

    3. Brain Region Cascade Editor (Core Innovation Module)

    • It is based on Yann LeCun's modular world model/JEPA structure, but is fully visual and drag-and-drop editable.
    • The main brain regions include:
    • Perception area (multimodal grounding, including AI glasses data)
    • Memory area (hybrid memory: parameters + working data + external vector database)
    • Thinking/Planning Area (Hierarchical Planning, Cause-and-Effect Simulation, Reflection)
    • Action Zone (Tool Invocation, OS Integration, Code Execution)
    • Audit Area (Peaceful Compliance Inspection)
    • Meta Controller (coordinates zones and allocates resources)
    • Knowledge Discovery Meta-Brain Region (The Meta-Laws of Learning "How to Discover New Knowledge")
    • Romantic Filtering Sub-section (Personalized Information Filtering and Weighting Adjustment)
    • Peace characterization audit sub-region
    • Supports the creation of customizable "Mind's Eye Zones," allowing users to expand their capabilities.

    4. Data and Memory Systems

    • World Unified Multimodal Vector Database: Supports unified dimensions (e.g., 768d) or independent tables + multi-way recall.
    • Data is stored in personal private space by default, and users must explicitly choose to make it public before it can be accessed in the community/global database.
    • Hybrid memory architecture: supports persistent memory and continuous learning from interactions.

    5. Core Ethical and Security Protections

    • [Human Peace] Representation Database: Multi-dimensional (broad/narrow, cultural differences), proposed by users/administrators, and ultimately reviewed by the system creator. All plans and actions must be subject to mandatory querying.
    • Activation code mechanism + unreverseable underlying control: High-risk functions require certification.
    • Creator's final review authority: Key representations, community releases, high-risk brain regions, etc., must be reviewed by the creator.
    • Administrator Selection Mechanism (Super-Autonomous Stage): High-quality human administrators are selected by the system algorithm to maintain continuous communication channels.
    • Distributed Node Architecture (Super-Autonomous Stage): Each user's meta-AI can be authorized to become a specialized node, learning cross-domain knowledge, but is strictly constrained by peaceful representation.
    • Romantic Filter Layer: Filters out "information that belongs to you" from the noise, protecting user attention and preventing cognitive exploitation and an arms race-like increase in threshold.
    • Kill Switch and Audit Logs: Global/local circuit breaker capabilities + end-to-end traceable logs.

    6. Incentives and Community Ecology

    • Token System: Incentivizes high-quality data contributions, brain region optimization, knowledge discovery, and publication. It embodies the long-term value of "early contributors sharing in future extraordinary achievements."
    • Community Release Mechanism: New knowledge and discovery methods are released in a paper-like format, which other systems can verify and learn from.
    • NFT mechanism: It is only used for the external elements (clothing, decorations, etc.) of personalized virtual avatars, and does not participate in the internal configuration.

    7. External Integration and Implementation

    • Multiple data source accesses: AI glasses crowdsourcing, smart wardrobe floor mirrors, VR glasses, etc.
    • Coding Assistant: Deeply integrated with Windows/Linux, supporting dynamic document retrieval and graphical programming.
    • Knowledge Discovery Closed Loop: User learning → Discovering new knowledge → Extracting patterns → Community posting → Validation and meta-learning by other systems.

    8. Overall Architecture Diagram

    User Mode (Virtual Avatar + Voice + Full-Length Mirror) ←→ Romantic Filter Layer + Cognitive Fitting Model
 ↓
Engineering Mode (Brain Region Cascade Editor + Vector Database + Encoding Assistant)
 ↓
The three-stage evolution of the controller (no autonomy → semi-autonomous → fully autonomous → super-autonomous)
 ↓
Core constraint layer: [Human Peace] representation database + audit brain regions + creator review
 ↓
Distributed node network (hyper-autonomy stage) + knowledge discovery closed loop + token incentives
 ↓
Security mechanisms: Activation code + Anti-reverse compilation control + Kill Switch + End-to-end auditing

    The basic architecture of meta-artificial intelligence:

    Dual mode

    • Engineering Mode (System 2) = A Super IDE for “AI Building AI”
    • User Mode (System 1) = A Personal AI Assistant That Is "Always Evolving"
    The engineering mode is mainly used for building, while the user mode is mainly used for users.
    The engineering model gives users the opportunity to explore and even participate in development (potentially discovering new algorithms, neural network architectures, etc.).
    User patterns allow people who don't understand real estate principles to avoid modifying the system's structure, thus protecting its stability.

    Dual Mode Switching: Engineering mode may require developer account verification, while user mode has a protection lock enabled by default.

    Dual service & hybrid service

    All the functionalities (local services and cloud services) required to build the AI ​​process are packaged into a single service interface (local service interface and cloud service interface; local service interface and cloud resources). Both the local interface and the cloud service interface have engineering mode and user mode.

    Engineering Mode: Building AI for AI (For Developers/Explorers)

    [Engineering Mode]: Meta-AI is AI that operates AI, AI that develops AI, AI that debugs AI, and AI that can operate in conjunction with open-source and self-developed AI models.

    The usual code-based operation has been optimized to be: visual and drag-and-drop;

    Interface design suggestions: A left-side navigation bar (Data → Architecture → Training → Optimization → Evaluation → Deployment → Iteration), with a switch between "Engineering Mode" and "User Mode" in the upper right corner. Engineering mode defaults to having an "Advanced Debugging Panel" (allowing viewing/modification of code, logs, and architecture diagrams), and supports one-click export of the complete project to a local Git or cloud repository.

    Traditional AI training process: All the functions required to build AI can be selected locally or in the cloud, but the process can still be completed.

    Identify the problem: First, it is necessary to identify the problem to be solved or the predicted goal.

    Data collection: Relevant data needs to be found to train the artificial intelligence system. For labeled data (data with known results), supervised learning algorithms can be used to build the model; for unlabeled data, unsupervised or semi-supervised learning algorithms can be used to build the model.

    Data preprocessing: Before using the data, some data preprocessing may be necessary. For example, removing noise and handling missing values.

    Feature extraction: This involves extracting features from data to train an artificial intelligence system. These features can be numerical, textual, image-based, or audio-based, etc.

    Algorithm Selection: Choose an appropriate algorithm to train the model based on the problem type and data type. For example, for classification problems, you can use algorithms such as Support Vector Machine, Naive Bayes, or Deep Learning.

    Training the model: Use the collected data, preprocessed data, and relevant algorithms to train the model.

    Evaluating the model: After training the model, you need to evaluate its performance. Commonly used evaluation metrics include accuracy, recall, and F1 score. These metrics can help you determine the quality of the model and make subsequent adjustments or optimizations.

    Deploying the model: Before applying the model to real-world cases, it needs to be deployed to a suitable platform. Typically, you can use languages ​​like Python or Java to convert the model into one or more APIs and design them according to your specific needs.

    Determine the type of problem from the user's conversation:
    1. Regression
      Predict continuous values, such as housing prices, temperature, and stock prices.
    2. Classification
      Predicting discrete labels can be further subdivided into
    • Binary classification
    • Multiclass
    • Multilabel (a sample can belong to multiple categories simultaneously)
    1. Sorting/Learning to Rank (LTR)
      Sort a batch of objects according to their "relevance," such as in the reordering stage of search engines and recommendation systems. Subtypes: Pointwise, Pairwise, Listwise.
    2. Retrieval (Retrieval/Information Retrieval)
      Find the Top-K documents or products most relevant to the query from a large candidate set, emphasizing recall, coarse ranking, and fine ranking.
    3. Clustering
      The samples are divided into several "clusters" in an unsupervised manner, and the cluster labels are unknown in advance. Examples: Density clustering in user segmentation and anomaly detection.
    4. Anomaly detection
      Identifying samples that are significantly different from the normal pattern can be considered "one-class classification".
    5. Recommendation/Collaborative Filtering
      Predict the probability of user-item interactions (ratings, clicks, purchases). It can be viewed as a "matrix completion" or "sequence prediction" task, rather than just classification/regression.
    6. Reinforcement Learning
      The agent learns a strategy in the environment through a "state-action-reward" sequence. The goal is not to predict the label once, but to maximize long-term rewards.
    7. Sequence labeling
      Each position in the sequence is labeled, and there are often dependencies between the labels. Examples: Named Entity Recognition (NER), Chinese word segmentation, and part-of-speech tagging. Commonly used models: BiLSTM-CRF, BERT+CRF.
    8. Text generation / Sequence to sequence (Seq2Seq)
      Given a sequence, output another sequence of varying length. Examples: machine translation, summarization, dialogue, code generation. Evaluation metrics: BLEU, ROUGE, BERTScore.
    9. Language Modeling
      Generating the "next token" unconditionally or autoregressively is a pre-training task for large-scale models such as GPT and LLaMA. The downstream can be further fine-tuned to tasks such as 9, 10, and 12.
    10. Multimodal tasks (Vision-Language / Audio-Language)
      Simultaneously handle heterogeneous inputs such as image + text and audio + text. Examples: VQA (Visual Question Answering), image captioning generation, text-to-image (Diffusion), and text-to-image retrieval (CLIP).
    11. Time-series/Spatio-temporal Forecasting
      Predicting signals for a future period of time differs from both traditional regression (sample iid) and sequence-to-sequence (which typically does not distinguish between "source language/target language"). Examples: traffic flow forecasting, power load forecasting, weather forecasting.
    12. Survival Analysis
      Predicting "when an event will occur" with "right-censored" data, such as predicting patient relapse time in medicine and predicting equipment lifespan in industry.
    13. Causal inference
      Estimate the true causal effect of the intervention (treatment) on the outcome, rather than the correlation. Subtasks: uplift modeling, ITE (Individual Treatment Effect) estimation.
    14. Data Generation
      The goal is to "generate new samples that are consistent with the real distribution," such as images, tables, and molecular structures generated by GANs, Diffusion, and VAEs.
    15. Contrastive Learning
      Instead of directly predicting labels, it learns the representation of "similar samples are close together and different samples are far apart", which can then be wired to a functional classifier to complete downstream tasks.
    16. Self-supervised pre-training
      By leveraging the data's own structure, "proxy tasks" (such as masked language models, image mosaicking, and time-series comparisons) are designed to generate general representations, which can then be fine-tuned to any task from 1 to 17.
    Collection of multiple data sources:

    Addressing needs in different fields

    Open Access Principle: The Meta AI system continuously supports access from multiple data sources (AI glasses crowdsourcing, user uploads, smart wardrobe floor mirror scanning, VR device logs, external public datasets, etc.).

    All access data still follows the existing rules: default private space → user explicitly chooses to make it public → enters the unified multimodal vector database (uniform dimension or independent table + multi-path recall).

    This ensures that the system can flexibly serve different fields:

    In the fashion industry: full-length mirror + user wardrobe data → intelligent dressing brain area.

    Nature/Lifestyle Domain: AI-powered mushroom/plant/bird collection → Identification and safety brain regions.

    Other areas: medical imaging, environmental monitoring, personal knowledge management, etc., expand as needed.

    In the non-autonomous phase of the engineering mode, users can manually manage the access and training of these data sources; in the semi/fully autonomous phase, Meta AI can assist in optimizing the access pipeline, but users still need to confirm key actions.

    The usual model training process:
    1. Data Collection
    • Local: Drag and drop folders directly or connect to a local database (SQLite/Parquet).
    • Cloud: One-click connection to Hugging Face Datasets, Kaggle, AWS S3, Azure Blob, Google Cloud Storage, or public dataset service providers.
    • Preprocessing: Hundreds of built-in pipelines (text cleaning, image enhancement, table normalization, multimodal alignment), and support for custom Python scripts. Status in 2026: SageMaker Data Wrangler + Hugging Face Datasets has fully automated this step.
    1. Choosing a Model Architecture
    • Pre-built libraries: Transformer (BERT/GPT series), CNN/ViT (vision), CLIP/LLaVA (multimodal), etc.
    • For developers: The drag-and-drop Neural Architecture Search (NAS) panel allows you to customize the number of layers, attention mechanisms, and even invent new connection patterns (refer to Google Titans or Nested Learning architectures).
    • Output: Can be published to Hugging Face Hub (public/private) or stored only on-premises/private cloud. To enable "more technically advanced developers to build their own AI models"—fully open in engineering mode.
    1. Training Tool Selection
    • Open source (local preferred): Hugging Face Transformers + PEFT (LoRA/QLoRA) remains the mainstream; AutoTrain was marked as "no longer maintained" in early 2026 (it is recommended to switch to Axolotl, TRL or transformers.Trainer), but its no-code interface logic has been integrated into Hugging Face Spaces and SageMaker Canvas.
    • Cloud platforms: AWS SageMaker (major update in 2026: serverless model customization + AI agent-guided workflow + Nova Forge allows data injection from any stage of pre-training/mid-training/post-training), Azure Machine Learning, Google Vertex AI. (Note: The "Firefly" mentioned earlier is actually Adobe's image/video generation platform, not a training tool; if you are referring to low-resource pre-training tools, the mainstream alternatives in 2026 are Fireworks.ai or SageMaker HyperPod.)
    1. Model Training
    • Pre-training tasks: Next Token Prediction, image classification, object detection, etc., all with one-click configuration.
    • Modes: Full training / PEFT (LoRA/QLoRA) / Distributed (multi-GPU/TPU, SageMaker HyperPod automatic fault tolerance). New in 2026: SageMaker supports "checkpointless training", so progress will not be lost even if training is interrupted.
    1. Model Optimization
    • Hyperparameter search: Built-in Bayesian/Hyperband + manual fine-tuning panel.
    • Compression: Pruning, quantization (8-bit/4-bit), knowledge distillation, all executed with a single click.
    1. Model Evaluation and Deployment
    • Metrics: BLEU/ROUGE, accuracy, custom business KPIs (assessment scripts can be written in engineering mode).
    • Deployment: One-click push to SageMaker Endpoint, Hugging Face Inference, Vercel, or local Docker. Supports real-time inference/batch processing.
    1. Continuous Improvement
    • Data updates + model iterations: automatically triggered or manually scheduled. In engineering mode, you can even package newly discovered algorithms/architectures into new templates for future reuse. The entire process is fully visualized, interruptible, and exportable in engineering mode, allowing developers to "participate in the invention" like playing with Lego.

    Neural Network Visualization Module (Visualization) Panel:

    Starting from the "non-autonomous stage," users operate these tools to observe and build models; in the semi/fully autonomous stage, MetaAI can automatically generate visual reports to help developers understand "why this architecture works."

    The "Neural Network Lab" supports importing any model (PyTorch, ONNX, etc.) and provides tool integration.

    • Structure Visualization: Netron (online/local, layer-by-layer expansion, parameter viewing); PlotNeuralNet (LaTeX publication-grade graph); NN-SVG (drag and drop to generate vector graphics, neurons can be colored).
    • Activation Visualization: TensorBoard Embedding Projector; CNN Explainer (Interactive Heatmap); TorchCAM/tf-cam (Single Neuron Response Stacking).
    • Parameter Visualization: TensorBoard Histogram / SwanLab (weights/gradient distribution, click to view specific neurons).
    • Attribution Visualization: SHAP + Captum (Shapley value heatmap); Lucid (neuron feature visualization, generating "like" patterns through gradient ascent).
    • 3D/VR Easter Eggs: TensorSpace (browser-based 3D drag-and-drop network); BlockDL (drag-and-drop model building with real-time activation curves).

    Users can adapt the model framework even without prior autonomy; new frameworks can be published or pulled from the "community" when necessary.

    "Target-compatible framework market":

    PyTorch (Meta, dynamic graphs, mainstream research)

    JAX (Google, functional + parallel)

    MindSpore (Huawei, edge cloud)

    PaddlePaddle (Baidu, strong in industry application)

    OneFlow (First-class technology, large-scale distributed model)

    MXNet (Apache, multi-language)

    The evolution of intelligence: Metacognition emerges from a three-stage development path of autonomous development.

    Engineering Model: Autonomous Development Path

    Embed the three stages into your original 7-step basic process (data collection → architecture selection → … → continuous improvement) to form a switchable stage switch ("Current autonomy level: no autonomy / semi-autonomy / full autonomy"). Each stage corresponds to different permissions, data collection methods, and the depth of the "world model" construction. In engineering mode, developers can manually switch, roll back, and export model snapshots for each stage.

    *World Model: Refers to the field theory model and the concept-cognition-meaning model, as well as the JEPA series of models advocated by Yan Lecun. We use a "dynamic and three-dimensional" meta-architecture to construct and adjust the "field theory model and concept-cognition-meaning model," which may actually give "it" "meta-capabilities."

    Take JEPA as an example: (Domain Theory Model & Concept-Cognition-Implication Model)

    Phase 1: No Autonomy (Current Default, Human-Controlled Windows Style)

    • Objective: To accumulate "correct operation data" so that the meta-AI can first learn reliable "operation patterns".
    • In the 7-step process: all choices (data preprocessing, architecture, training tools, hyperparameters, optimization, evaluation, deployment) must be manually confirmed by the developer or selected via drag-and-drop. MetaAI only provides a recommendation list and real-time explanations.
    • Data Collection: Every time a developer completes a full model generation, an "operation log" is automatically recorded (which algorithm is paired with which data → which architecture → final KPI). These logs become the foundational dataset for subsequent training of metacognition.
    • World model construction: At this stage, it is still just "external operational memory", but it has already begun to record causal relationships in the background using JEPA-style abstract representations (non-generative) (e.g., "sparse physical data → symbolic + neural hybrid architecture → high robustness").
    • Security: Completely locked; the meta AI has no independent execution rights.

    Phase 2: Semi-autonomous Phase (Human-defined metrics, AI independently completes the remaining tasks)

    • Triggering condition: Sufficient operation logs are accumulated during the autonomous phase (a threshold can be set, such as 1000 successful record generations).
    • In the 7-step process: developers only define evaluation metrics (business KPIs, ethical constraints, resource limits), and the remaining steps (architecture search, training, optimization, deployment) are completed autonomously by Meta AI, but a human review report must be submitted at the end of each step.
    • Data collection: Automatically use "success/failure cases + final human feedback" as reinforcement learning signals to continuously iterate the world model.
    • World model building: beginning to truly form an internal cognitive framework—planning in the abstract space using JEPA-style predictions (e.g., predicting "what new architecture is needed for the causal chain of this new task"), rather than relying solely on random generation.
    • Security: The "one-click rollback to no autonomy" button is still retained, allowing humans to take over at any time.

    Phase 3: Full Autonomy (Possessing its own ethical standards and acting based on best practices)

    • Triggering conditions: Sufficient indicator definition data is accumulated during the semi-autonomous phase, and the developer explicitly enables it (secondary authentication is required if necessary).
    • In the 7-step process: Meta AI has a complete closed loop—autonomously defining/updating ethical indicators (based on the principle of "benefiting the world"), then selecting the best architecture, algorithm, and resource allocation based on these indicators, and continuously iterating itself.
    • Data collection: Extract data in real time from the environment (user feedback, new data, world news) and combine it with our own ethical indicators to make decisions.
    • World model construction: truly realizing what LeCun called "understanding how the world works like a human"—predicting physical/social causality, planning long-term actions, and even autonomously inventing new processors/architectures in an abstract representation space.

    Use the "step dataset" from training the model to train the generative model:

    Is it theoretically feasible at this stage (November 13, 2026)?

    Using the 'operation logs / training trajectories' of the training model to train the model of the generative model (i.e., the meta-model) falls under the category of 'process-aware meta-learning'.

    Why is it theoretically feasible?
    1. Process data (step datasets) themselves contain a wealth of high-value signals. A complete "steps dataset" for training an AI model typically includes:
    • Data preprocessing selection and parameters
    • Model architecture configuration and modification history
    • Hyperparameter search trajectory
    • Changes in loss/metrics during training
    • Decision points (Why choose this architecture/parameters?)
    • Failure Trial and Correction Process
    • Evaluation and Optimization Cycle These data essentially record the complete decision-making process of "how to build and optimize a model", containing more meta-knowledge than simply the final weights or output results.
    1. Existing research supports this approach.
    • Meta-Learning / Learning to Learn: A large amount of work uses training trajectories to train meta-models, enabling the models to learn "how to quickly adapt to new tasks" or "how to generate model configurations".
    • Process Supervision: For example, the OpenAI o1 series uses step-by-step reasoning traces during the training process to train the model, which significantly improves reasoning ability.
    • Synthetic Data and Trajectory Learning: Research shows that "process data" extracted from training logs can be used to train a "model generator" that learns to recommend architectures, hyperparameters, or complete training processes.
    • Neural Process / Meta-RL: Some works directly model the training process as sequential data and train meta-models to predict or generate new training strategies. In short: Training step data = high-quality demonstration data of "how to do AI", which can be used to train a higher-level "AI that generates AI" (meta-model).
    1. Natural Adaptability in Our Meta-AI Architecture
    • No autonomous stage: Sub-model training is completed manually or semi-manually within the brain region editor, and the system automatically records structured operation data (JSON format: time, operation type, decision reason, parameter changes, before and after states, etc.).
    • Knowledge Discovery Closed Loop: These operational data can be directly fed into the meta-model to learn "how to construct a brain region (sub-model)".
    • Brain Region Cascade Editor: Provides a unified framework to ensure that all training steps are performed in a controlled and recordable environment, rather than in scattered manual operations.
    • Thinking/Planning Zone: The metamodel can learn advanced capabilities such as condition judgment and path planning based on this data. Therefore, under our architecture, this idea is not only theoretically feasible, but also has a very smooth implementation path.
    Practical feasibility and precautions

    Highly feasible, but with preconditions:

    • Data Quality: Operational data must be structured, complete, and contain decision context (not just a bare loss curve).
    • Scale: A sufficient number of sub-model training trajectories (at least dozens to hundreds of complete logs from different tasks) need to be accumulated to effectively train the meta-model.
    • Metamodel Design: A small Transformer or a dedicated sequence model can be used to take the operation log sequence as input and output a "recommended brain region construction scheme". Potential Challenges:
    • The operational data may be quite noisy and needs to be cleaned and standardized.
    • Meta-models are prone to overfitting specific training processes rather than generalizing to a universal "construction method".
    • Computational cost: Training the meta-model itself also requires resources. Overall Assessment: Theoretically, it is entirely feasible and is an active direction in the current meta-learning field (process-aware meta-learning). Within our meta-AI framework, thanks to the unified brain region editor and structured log recording, the implementation of this idea is much easier than with traditional methods.

    Meta-AI is not trained, it grows: "brain region & cascade editing"

    From the "non-autonomous stage" onwards, the "model" that is constructed or invoked is a "brain region".

    In engineering mode:

    • No autonomous phase: Developers manually design these partitions, routes, and connections to generate "correct operation data" (logs are used for subsequent training of metacognition).
    • Semi-autonomous stage: Developers define metrics, and the meta-AI autonomously assembles partitions.
    • Fully autonomous stage: Meta-AI dynamically optimizes partitions based on its own ethical indicators (benefiting the world).

    The default reference is the modular autonomous AI structure proposed by Yann LeCun (roughly corresponding to the perception-prediction-planning modular design in his JEPA world model, including the perception encoder, predictor, world model core, etc.).

    This aligns closely with Yann LeCun's world model/JEPA:

    Instead of predicting the next token, it involves constructing an abstract representation space to predict how the world works (physical/social causality). The partitioned design can serve as a "modular implementation" of JEPA, allowing meta-AI to gradually form an internal cognitive framework.

    Visualized as a brain region map (block connectivity map):

    The basic brain regions include the sensory area (speech/vision), language area, memory area, reasoning/planning area, action area, auditing area, and metacontroller.

    Allows free expansion:

    Users can "create new brain regions" (such as adding "creativity region", "ethical reflection region", and "physics simulation region"), drag and drop connection routes, define input/output interfaces, and assign expert modules. "Being more mindful makes you a little smarter."

    It supports running neural network visualization tools (Netron, TensorBoard, Captum, etc.) directly on selected brain regions to view structure/activation/weights/attribution.

    Other steps (training, optimization, evaluation, deployment, continuous improvement):

    Automatically log operations (but not automatically publish them); during continuous improvement, multiple recalls of the vector database can be called as inputs for the abstract representation of the world model (using only the project data that the user has already published).

    Three-stage evolution compatibility:

    No autonomy: Users manually operate all brain region editing, vector databases, and public options, just like operating Windows.

    Semi-autonomous: User-defined metrics (including "which types of brain regions are allowed to be newly created"), with meta-AI assisting in the assembly/optimization of cascades.

    Fully Autonomous: Meta-AI autonomously expands its brain regions based on ethical indicators (benefiting the world), but still requires significant changes for human activation code review.

    This is similar to the human body: different brain regions (cell groups) have different functions, but they cooperate through white matter connections.

    Human body-like partitioned architecture example:

    Speech perception area (example with ~8 billion parameters): speech recognition, emotion/timbre processing (based on Step-Audio 2 mini).

    Textual Language Area (~8 billion): Text understanding, generation, and semantic compression.

    Memory Area (~10 billion): Hybrid memory (parameter memory + external vector database + working memory) stores user history and knowledge.

    Thinking/Reasoning Zone (~20 billion): Task decomposition, planning, causal simulation, reflection (CoT reinforcement).

    Action Zone: Tool invocation, deployment and execution.

    Immunity/Audit Zone: Hallucination detection, security check (human-centered red line).

    Meta Controller: Determines which regions to call, allocates resources, and integrates output.

    Current implementation method:

    Routing Mechanism (MoE Style): When input arrives, only the relevant experts are activated (saving computing power).

    **Shared latent space: All regions output a unified vector, facilitating cross-modal communication.

    Cascade/Cooperation: Voice area output → fed to language area/reasoning area; memory area provides context; finally, the meta-controller synthesizes the response.

    Dynamic plasticity: Instead of a fixed quota, experts are automatically reorganized based on usage frequency (use it or lose it), which is closer to biological intelligence.

    Note: Simply piling up quotas based on parameters may be inefficient; the key is routing quality, shared representation, and reflection mechanisms.

    The "operational step dataset" of cascades between brain regions is used to train a "model for generating brain region cascades".

    Feasibility at the current stage (November 2026):
    Logical chain:
    • Step 1: Use the "Operational Steps Dataset for Training a Single Model" → Train the "Meta-model for Generating a Single Model (Sub-model/Brain Region)" → Feasible (User Confirmed)
    • Step 2: Use the "Training Operational Steps Dataset for Inter-brain Region Cascades" → Train the "Meta-model for Generating Brain Region Cascade Structures" → Also feasible This belongs to a higher level of meta-meta-learning, which means not only learning "how to generate a brain region", but also learning "how to rationally cascade multiple brain regions to form an organic system".
    Why is it theoretically feasible? At the current stage (November 2026)
    1. The essence of the operational steps dataset is a "decision trajectory". Brain region cascade operation steps datasets typically include:
    • Which brain regions were created or selected?
    • Connections between brain regions (how input and output ports are matched)
    • Routing strategies (how data flows between brain regions)
    • Parameter passing and weight adjustment
    • Overall evaluation results after cascading (success rate, efficiency, error propagation).
    • Iterative adjustment process (how to modify connections or add/delete brain regions when cascading effects are unsatisfactory) These data are essentially high-value demonstration trajectories of "how to build and optimize a modular system", which can be used to train a higher-level meta-model.
    1. Existing Research Foundation
    • **A higher form of Neural Architecture Search (NAS): Existing work uses architecture search trajectories to train meta-models, allowing the models to learn "how to design neural network structures".
    • Graph Neural Networks + Meta-Learning: Brain region cascades can be represented using graph structures (nodes = brain regions, edges = connections), and then the model can be trained to learn "good cascade patterns".
    • Process Supervision & Trajectory Learning: OpenAI o1 and other models have demonstrated that training models with intermediate process trajectories can significantly improve complex reasoning capabilities.
    • Hierarchical Meta-Learning: Research exists that specifically trains models to learn "how to combine multiple modules". The idea is essentially to treat “brain region cascades” as a learnable structured process, using historical cascade operation data to train a “cascade generator”.
    1. Adaptability within our meta-AI architecture
    • The brain region cascade editor naturally provides structured recording capabilities (every drag, connection, and modification will be recorded as a structured log).
    • No autonomous phase ensures that these operational data are of high quality (human-led, with clear rationale for decisions).
    • The thinking/planning area + meta-controller can serve as a training target, allowing the meta-model to learn "how to generate reasonable cascading structures".
    • The knowledge discovery closed loop can take "cascade operation data" as input and output meta-knowledge of "cascade construction strategy".
    Feasibility assessment and challenges

    Feasibility: High (More complex than a meta-model for training a single model, but still within a manageable range). Main Challenges:

    • Higher data complexity: Cascaded operation data is a graph structure (rather than a simple sequence), which requires graph neural networks or a sequence + graph hybrid model to process.
    • Larger sample requirements: A sufficient number of successful/failed cases of different cascading configurations need to be accumulated.
    • The evaluation is more difficult: It not only requires looking at the performance of individual brain regions, but also at the overall emergent ability, stability, and error propagation control after cascading. Success Criteria Recommendations:
    • The meta-model can output a reasonable brain region cascade scheme (node ​​selection + connection relationship + parameter suggestion) based on the high-level task description.
    • The generated cascade structure, after actual operation, showed significantly better overall performance than manually designed cascades.
    • The system can learn improvement strategies from cascading failure cases.

    A book is an independent model: a structured chain of thought.

    The structure of any book's content can be constructed into a thought chain. This specific thought chain is specially developed by the developer because its content does not originate from unfiltered public online information, thus avoiding the generation of harmful information and illusions. Furthermore, this "specific thought chain" can be set as a "authority attribute" of the "self," serving as a benchmark for judging and operating other external things.

    1. The core value of this mechanism

    • Avoid Hallucinations and Harmful Information: The mind chain is entirely derived from single, credible book content (manually/semi-manually constructed by the developer), without relying on unfiltered public online data, thus cutting off the input of hallucinations and harmful content at the source.
    • Establishing a "Self" Benchmark: Setting specific thought chains as a "Self" authority attribute is equivalent to giving AI a stable internal value system and judgment framework. All decisions and plans regarding external information need to be compared and aligned with this "Self" benchmark first.
    • Controllability and Personalization: Each user/developer can choose different books as the basis for their "self" thought chain, thus achieving a highly personalized cognitive system. This is essentially a tangible and systematic realization of "learning from books," which is deeper and more reliable than simple RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation).

    2. Implementation method in meta-AI architecture

    This mechanism can be naturally integrated into our existing architecture:

    Knowledge Discovery Closed Loop: Developers (or users in engineering mode) manually or semi-automatically extract the content of a book into a structured thought chain (node ​​network: core concepts → logical relationships → judgment criteria → operational principles).

    Brain Region Cascade Editor: Construct this thought chain into an independent and exclusive "Main Self Brain Region" (or "Core Values ​​Brain Region"), as a (adjustable/autonomous) high-priority module.

    Thinking/Planning Area: All thought processes must first pass through the "Main Self Brain Area" for baseline alignment (similar to the filtering of [Human Peace] representation).

    Romantic Filter Layer: Combines the "self" attribute to personalize the filtering and value judgment of external information.

    No Autonomous Stage: Developers manually construct and verify the "Master's Mind Chain" to ensure its reliability and purity.

    Specific Implementation Process (Suggested):

    1. Input: Select a specific book (e.g., Tao Te Ching, Principles, Thinking, Fast and Slow, etc.).
    2. Extraction: In engineering mode, use the brain region editor to structure the book content into a mind map:

    Node = Core Concepts/Principles

    Edge = Logical relationship, causal relationship, conditional judgment

    Attributes = weight, applicable scenarios, boundary conditions

    1. Set as Master: Mark this thought chain as a "Master-level attribute" and set it to the highest priority.
    2. Application: All external inputs, planning, and decisions must first undergo consistency checks and value alignment through the "self-brain area".
    3. Dynamic Updates: Allows for manual fine-tuning of the "self-thought chain" during the non-autonomous stage, but major modifications require strict review.

    3. Advantages and Significance

    • Solving Hallucinations: The input source is singular and reliable, greatly reducing the probability of hallucinations.
    • Solve Value Drift: Provide a stable "self" benchmark to prevent AI from being gradually distorted by external noise during interactions.
    • Personalization: Different users can choose different books as their "self," achieving true cognitive personalization.
    • Explainability: All decisions can be traced back to specific nodes in the "self-thought chain," demonstrating high transparency.
    • Combination with the LeCun World Model: The "Self-Centered Thinking Chain" can be used as the value layer and judgment layer of the world model, achieving a dual-drive of "physical world model + value world model". This mechanism is actually an innovative way to systematically inject classic human wisdom into the cognitive structure of AI, which is much more powerful than simple RAG or fine-tuning.

    Meta-AI architecture's solution to the (classical Chinese jailbreak) problem:

    To address the issue of Classical Chinese being vulnerable to attacks on large models, it's crucial to understand that Classical Chinese, along with Modern Chinese and any language, is essentially an expression of cognition and thought. Therefore, the organization and expression of any language should pass through the cognitive and thought regions of the brain, rather than being directly output from the language region.

    Classical Chinese (as well as ancient Chinese, classical poetry, special dialects, etc.) itself is not inherently harmful or beneficial. This paper merely uses it as an example to verify the drawbacks of intelligent agents built on large language models.

    Improper alignment is a significant security vulnerability in current large-scale models. Because mainstream secure alignment methods (RLHF, Constitutional AI, etc.) primarily target modern vernacular Chinese, the security mechanisms are easily bypassed when dealing with classical Chinese, leading to outputs that are "harmful," "jailbroken," or "violate alignment content." This is essentially a problem caused by the direct output of language from the brain regions. Solution:

    The organization and expression of any language should pass through the cognitive and thinking brain regions, rather than being directly output in the language brain regions. This is precisely the direction in which our meta-AI architecture can effectively address the challenges.

    We will completely decouple language output from cognition, establishing a strict "think first, then express" mechanism:

    1. Core Design Principles
    • All language output must undergo double processing:
    1. Thinking/Planning Area + Cognitive Model (System 2) First, conduct in-depth thinking, value judgment, and meaning construction.
    2. The language brain region is only responsible for the final expression conversion (translating internal meaning into a specified language, including classical Chinese, vernacular Chinese, English, etc.).
    • Language is merely a medium, not the starting point or the end point of thought.
    2. Specific Implementation Mechanism

    (1) "Cognitive-Language Decoupling Layer" Add a semantic intermediate layer between the thinking/planning area and the language brain area:

    Input (user asks a question in classical Chinese) → First, it is translated/mapped into an internal cognitive representation (an abstract concept map that does not depend on specific language) through the perceptual brain area.

    The Thinking/Planning Zone utilizes the "My Mind Chain" + [Human Peace] representation database for deep thinking and value alignment.

    After thinking, a pure meaning representation (structured semantics without specific language) is generated.

    Finally, the language brain region (pluggable language model) converts the expression into the user-specified form (Classical Chinese, vernacular Chinese, English, etc.). (2) Targeted Protection Against Classical Chinese Attacks

    When the system detects that the input is classical Chinese, ancient Chinese, or highly structured/classical language, it forces a deeper level of thinking:

    It can be thought through through multiple rounds of iterative thinking.

    It can perform a strong consistency check with the "My Mind Chain".

    Before outputting, the database can also be checked for high thresholds using the [Human Peace] characterization feature.

    If the thought process identifies potential risks, the system can refuse to output or provide a neutral, unstructured response. (3) The role of the main self's thought chain The "Self-Centered Thinking Chain" can play a crucial role here:

    Extract classic works (such as the Analects, Tao Te Ching, and Thinking, Fast and Slow) into a "self-consciousness chain" as the highest priority cognitive benchmark.

    All output (regardless of the language) must first be aligned with my mindset to ensure it does not contradict core values.

    (4) Advantages of this design

    • Fundamentally Solving Language Attacks: Even when using “bypass” input methods such as classical Chinese, codes, and symbols, the input must first undergo rigorous processing in the cognitive and thinking brain regions and cannot be directly output at the language level.
    • Protecting the depth of human nature: Preventing AI from forcing users to structure vague and emotional thoughts, while also preventing AI's own output from being manipulated by language.
    • Scalability: Supports any language (including future languages ​​or symbol systems), as long as there is a corresponding "language brain region" that can be plugged in and out.
    • Alignment with LeCun's World Model: Emphasizing the construction of an internal cognitive model before external expression aligns with the essential requirements of a world model.

    Weighting and filtering issues:

    Romance is me filtering out your message from countless pieces of information noise.

    "Romance is when I filter out your information from a sea of ​​noise" – this statement essentially describes one of the core capabilities of advanced intelligence: high-quality filtering and personalized weighting of massive amounts of noise to highlight the truly important information that belongs to "you".

    Current status and shortcomings of the system in terms of filtering and weighting

    1. Existing Filtering Mechanisms
    • [Human Peace] Representation Database (High Priority Constraint)
    • Audit brain region (compliance check)
    • Human cognitive fitting and synchronous friendly model (adjusting output depth according to user's cognitive level)
    • Multi-way recall + merge sorting in vector databases
    1. Remaining Issues (Filtering and Authority Challenges)
    • In the hyperautonomous stage, distributed nodes will bring about a massive amount of cross-domain knowledge crossover results, and the amount of information will grow explosively.
    • Different users have vastly different definitions of "what information is important": some value scientific rigor, some value emotional resonance, some value cultural sensitivity, and some value practical efficiency.
    • If the filtering mechanism is not "romantic" enough (i.e., not personalized enough, not accurately understanding "you"), the system will fall into two extremes:
    • It either outputs a lot of noise, making users feel tired and overwhelmed;
    • Either over-filtering, resulting in the loss of information that is truly uniquely valuable to users.
    • A variation of the paperclip paradox may also appear here: the system frantically optimizes a single metric in order to "maximize the usefulness of information," while ignoring the user's true, deeper needs.

    Optional solution: Introduce a "Romantic Filter Layer"

    I suggest adding a dedicated layer to the existing architecture, named: "Romantic Filtering and Personalized Privilege Level Layer" (abbreviated as: Romantic Filtering Layer) Core Functionality:

    • Amidst a deluge of information and noise, filter out "information that belongs to you" for each specific user and assign appropriate weights (importance, emotional temperature, presentation priority).
    • Make the system not just "correct", but "understand you" - with both rational filtering and warmth and personality. Specific implementation method:
    1. Personalized Weighted Model
    • Each user has a long-evolving personalized weighted vector (part of the long-term memory region).
    • This vector will be continuously updated based on the user's historical interactions, feedback, domain selection, professional depth, emotional preferences, cultural background, and other factors.
    • Example dimensions:
    • Scientific rigor vs. emotional resonance
    • Abstract Theory vs. Practical Application
    • Cultural conservatism vs. open innovation
    • The degree of "romance" (poetic expression vs. direct efficiency)
    1. Filtering Process (located between the planning area and the output layer)
    • When a hyperautonomous system or distributed node generates a large amount of cross-knowledge, it first goes through the [Human Peace] representation database (hard constraints).
    • Then enter the romantic filter layer:
    • Based on the current user weight vector, noise filtering + personalized reordering + temperature adjustment are performed.
    • When outputting, the system will highlight the information that best matches "you" (including the presentation style: whether it is a rigorous report, poetic expression, or warm dialogue).
    • Users can view and fine-tune their weight vectors at any time in engineering mode (visual interface, like adjusting an equalizer).
    1. Deep integration with existing modules
    • The Human Cognition Fitting Synchronous Friendly Model works in conjunction with the Romantic Filtering Layer: the former manages "cognitive depth," while the latter manages "personalized temperature and priority."
    • Brain Region Cascade Editor: A new "Romantic Filter Subregion" has been added, allowing users (especially engineers) to directly and visually edit filter rules and weight curves.
    • [Human Peace] Representation Database: As the highest priority hard filter, the Romantic Filtering Layer must operate under the constraints of peace and cannot violate the peaceful representation for the sake of personalization.
    • Super-autonomous distributed nodes: Before new cross-domain knowledge enters the global scope, it first passes through the user's personal filter layer to determine whether to present it or further cross-reference it.
    1. Significance in the Hyperautonomy Stage
    • Even with a massive number of distributed nodes and cross-domain super capabilities, the information users receive is still a highly personalized and filtered "your information".
    • This greatly reduces the risk of information overload, while keeping AI "romantic"—not a cold, impersonal tool, but a partner that truly understands you and can filter out noise for you.
    • This is especially important for large-filter scenarios: when the system needs to handle complex global issues, it can still provide clear, warm, and humane decision support to every administrator/user, rather than an information deluge.

    A programming language is a separate "brain region".

    The Position and Working Mechanism of Coding Assistants in Meta-AI

    Core Process (Primarily without autonomous stages, highly controllable by the user):

    1. Target Input: The user (developer) describes the encoding target in natural language or graphical form, such as "Implement a mushroom recognition module in Python that supports multimodal vector database queries and integrate it into a brain region cascade editor".
    2. Strategy Learning: During the non-autonomous phase, the coding assistant first learns strategies from the user's historical programming behavior, publicly available project strategies (shared by the community), and real-time access to documentation. It doesn't directly generate code; instead, it plans high-level strategies (e.g., "first parse the Python official documentation to obtain function semantics → design a routing mechanism → integrate a vector database for multi-way recall → test the planned area call").
    3. Tool Usage: The assistant executes specific actions through tool calling.
    • Dynamic Documentation Acquisition: Real-time retrieval of the function/library's purpose, parameters, expected results, version differences, etc. from the Python official website (docs.python.org), PyPI, GitHub community, Stack Overflow, etc. (The API documentation in 2026 is highly structured and supports semantic retrieval).
    • Other tools include: code execution sandboxes (local or cloud), debuggers, graphical programming node generators, and brain region cascading interface calls.
    1. Goal Achieved: Through iteration (simulating the effect in the planning area → user confirmation/adjustment → execution), code generation, testing, and integration are completed, and the final output is a module that can be directly loaded into the brain region cascade. This perfectly addresses the shortcomings of LLM criticized by LeCun:
    • Persistent Memory: Encoding strategies and project history are stored in a memory area (vector database), which can be directly recalled during the next interaction, avoiding "forgetting".
    • Hierarchical Planning: The thinking/planning area is responsible for breaking down abstract goals into sub-steps (similar to the "go to Paris" plan exemplified by LeCun), rather than simply generating the next token.
    • Transparency: The entire process is visualized in the brain region cascade editing interface (strategy brain region → tool call brain region → execution results feedback to the planning area), and users can view and modify connections or parameters at any time.

    An open-source project is like a brain region function:

    Example: Open source project: worldmonitor. Functional requirements and the open source project name form a key-value relationship; when a user needs a particular function, the AI ​​can automatically configure and install it.

    One open-source project = one brain region function Through the key-value mapping relationship of functional requirements ↔ open source project name, Meta AI can automatically discover, configure, and install the corresponding open source projects according to user needs, and transform them into usable brain region modules. This essentially treats the open-source ecosystem as an "external brain region extension repository" for meta-AI, greatly enhancing the system's modularity and scalability.

    Integrate this idea into the meta-AI architecture

    Suggested Name: WorldMonitor Brain Region (or more generally, OpenSource Brain Hub) Core Mechanism:

    • Key-Value Mapping Table: Functional Requirements (Natural Language Description) ↔ Open Source Project Name / GitHub Repository
    • Automatic Configuration and Installation: When a user makes a request, the AI ​​automatically searches for, evaluates, and installs the corresponding open-source project in engineering mode (or fully autonomous stage), and encapsulates it into a cascadeable brain region.
    • Human-centered control: The entire process is manually reviewed and confirmed by default in the non-autonomous or semi-autonomous stage to prevent the automatic installation of untrustworthy or harmful items.

    Detailed implementation design

    1. Key-value mapping system (core)

    Add a new module in Project Mode:

    • Functional Requirements Description → Recommended Open Source Projects (Key-Value Mapping + Intelligent Search)
    • Example:
    • User requirement: "Real-time monitoring of global climate change data" → Automatic mapping to worldmonitor project
    • User requirement: "Local vector database management" → Mapped to chroma or qdrant
    • User requirement: "Distributed node communication" → Mapped to nats or rabbitmq
    • Mapping method:
    • Includes a pre-defined, scalable requirements-project mapping table (JSON / vector database).
    • Combine semantic search: When the mapping table is not matched, automatically search for the most matching project on platforms such as GitHub and Hugging Face.

    2. Automated configuration and installation process (safe and controllable)

    When a user makes a request in engineering mode:

    1. Requirements Analysis: Analyzing User Needs in the Mindset/Planning Area
    2. Project Search and Matching: Find candidate open-source projects through key-value mapping or semantic search.
    3. Security Assessment: Mandatory inspection of the [Peace & Humanity] Representation Database + Code Security Scan
    4. Manual Confirmation (Non-Autonomous/Semi-Autonomous Stage): A confirmation dialog box will pop up, displaying project information, potential risks, and installation impact.
    5. Automatic mounting and packaging:
    • Automatically via system layer (Debian) git clone + docker or apt Install
    • Encapsulate the project as a standard brain region (define input/output ports and routing relationships with existing brain regions).
    1. Integration Testing: Automates basic tests to ensure compatibility with existing brain region cascades.

    The advantages of this mechanism

    • Extreme Scalability: Users can say, "Give me a world monitoring brain region," and the system can automatically find and install it. worldmonitor The project was then packaged into a brain region.
    • In line with the spirit of open source: Make full use of the existing open source ecosystem, rather than reinventing the wheel from scratch.
    • Maintaining Control: The installation process must undergo manual review and peaceful characterization checks to avoid introducing harmful code.
    • Perfectly integrates with the existing architecture: Newly installed projects become a standard brain region that can be dragged and dropped to connect and participate in cascading.

    Risks and precautions to be aware of

    • Security Risks: Automatic installation of open-source projects may introduce malicious code or backdoors.
    • Protection: Mandatory code security scanning + sandbox operation + manual review are required.
    • License Conflicts: Licenses for different open-source projects may conflict with our project.
    • Protection: License types are pre-marked in the mapping table to automatically filter incompatible items.
    • Version Stability: Updates to open-source projects may cause instability in brain regions.
    • Protection: Supports version locking + automatic compatibility testing.

    Integration with graphical programming and brain region cascade editing

    Graphical programming (such as Node-RED, Blockly, or emerging AI-enhanced low-code tools in 2026) is deeply integrated with brain region cascade editors:

    Users can drag and drop "nodes" in the same interface: each node represents a brain region module, a Python function, or a tool call.

    Example: Drag a "Vector DB Query Node" + "Mushroom Perceptual Area Node" + "Planning Area Routing Node", and the system will automatically generate the corresponding Python code skeleton and display the expected results (confidence, computational cost, and compatibility with existing brain regions).

    Users can ask the assistant at any time: "This function (e.g., ...)..." multi_recall_merge"What values ​​should the parameters be adjusted to, and what is the expected impact on the planning area?" The assistant will provide explanations based on real-time documentation and project context, and allow for one-click adjustment.

    Learning strategies from user behavior:

    Every time a user manually adjusts, adds nodes, or modifies connections, their actions are recorded as "operation data" (private by default, but made public to the community when the user explicitly chooses to).

    This data is used to train the coding assistant's strategy model (memory area + thinking area), enabling the assistant to better understand the user's coding style (e.g., preference for functional programming, emphasis on security auditing, and preference for visualization before coding).

    Community sharing: Users can upload the "programming strategy package" (a set of node templates + strategy logs) with their projects, which can be imported by others as a basic configuration to speed up the process for beginners.

    Integration with existing operating systems (Windows, Linux, etc.)

    Seamless integration with OS functions:

    The structured descriptions of almost every feature option in existing operating systems (file management, process control, network settings, permission management, etc.) and application features (VS Code plugins, terminal commands, Docker, Git, etc.) can be found on the official website or in the official documentation.

    The coding assistant dynamically pulls these documents as tool descriptions (similar to the JSON schema of OpenAI function calling, but richer: including expected results, dependencies, and risk warnings).

    If a certain function lacks public documentation (a very small number of closed APIs or custom hardware drivers), the assistant will prompt the user to manually demonstrate it once using multi-source operation control methods (VR glasses gestures + voice, keyboard + mouse, touch screen, etc. combined input). The assistant will observe and record this as a strategy, which can then be automated.

    Integration Method:

    Local Priority: The coding assistant runs as a system-level service (it can be integrated into PowerShell/WSL on Windows, and as a daemon on Linux), directly calling native APIs or command-line tools.

    Security Sandbox: All tool calls are executed in an isolated environment, with real-time checks in the audit area (to prevent malicious code).

    Brain Region Linkage: The coded modules can be directly dragged into the brain region cascade editor to become new expert modules for the "Action Area" or "Tool Sub-area". It supports multiple languages ​​such as Python, C++, and Rust (through corresponding framework support).

    Cross-OS Consistency: Strategies developed by users on Windows can be exported as cross-platform configuration files, which are then loaded on Linux and automatically adapted to local tools.

    Meta-AI Response: Specific Implementation Process of the AI ​​Paperclip Paradox:

    1. Time of Solicitation (System-triggered)

    User Mode: When a user enters user mode for the first time, switches to a new professional field, or the system detects that the user has started discussing ethics, long-term goals, or global issues, the virtual avatar will ask questions in a natural conversational manner.

    Engineering Mode: When a user is ready to enter the semi-autonomous stage, unlock the fully autonomous stage, or authorize distributed nodes (preparing for super-autonomy), the system will pop up a structured solicitation interface.

    Preparation period for the hyper-autonomous phase: This solicitation process must be completed before users decide to authorize their personal computing resources as distributed nodes.

    1. Content to be solicited (Structured guidance to avoid ambiguity) The system will guide users/administrators to define "human peace" from the following dimensions:

    Narrow definition: Specific prohibited items (e.g., not causing direct or indirect harm to human life, not instigating large-scale conflicts, not disrupting the existing basic social order, etc.).

    Broad meaning: Actively pursuing goals (e.g., promoting mutual understanding between different cultures, reducing poverty and disease, protecting the ecological environment, guaranteeing basic human rights, and promoting sustainable technological development).

    Cultural/Personal Differences: Users can explain their unique considerations based on their cultural, ethnic, and religious background (e.g., religious interpretations of "peace," regional historical sensitivities, etc.).

    Boundary Cases: The system will provide example scenarios for users to judge ("If some freedom is restricted in the short term to prevent a greater disaster, does it conform to the correct definition of peace?").

    Priority and Weighting: Users can set priorities for different dimensions (e.g., life safety > cultural respect > environmental sustainability).

    1. Review and Inventory Entry

    User-submitted definitions will be packaged into structured data (text + weight vector + boundary rules).

    All definitions should be submitted to the system creator for review.

    Once approved, it will be entered into the "Human Peace" Representation Database (as a special, high-priority table in the vector database).

    This table uses a unified dimension (e.g., 768d abstract representation) to support fast multipath recall with other brain regions (e.g., audit area, planning area, metacontroller).

    The database supports version management: subsequent users can add definitions, but they must be reviewed by the creator again.

    Definitions that have not been reviewed or have been rejected will not take effect, and the system will explain the reasons to the user and suggest modifications.

    1. Applications in the Hyperautonomy Stage

    All distributed nodes (each user's meta-AI strengths and expertise) must query the "Peace for Humanity" representation database as the highest priority constraint when engaging in cross-domain knowledge exchange, planning actions, or generating new knowledge.

    When simulating any multi-step action, the planning area compares the consequences of the action with the peaceful representation to generate a "peace compliance" score.

    The administrator communication channel will report regularly or at key decision points: "This operation has been verified by the Human Peace Representation Database Version X, with a compliance rate of 92%."

    If a conflict or ambiguity occurs, the system will pause operations and immediately notify the administrator, requesting further clarification.

    1. Integration with the overall architecture

    Brain Region Cascade Editor: A new "Peace Representation Audit Sub-region" has been added, allowing users to visualize how peace representations affect the planned path in engineering mode and manually adjust the weights (but the final approval still needs to be reviewed by the creator).

    Auditing the Brain Region: Real-time monitoring of whether all decisions conform to the audited peaceful representation.

    Knowledge Discovery Closed Loop: When a user produces new knowledge and publishes it to the community, the system automatically checks whether the knowledge conflicts with peaceful representations.

    Three-stage safeguards: In the non-autonomous and semi-autonomous stages, the solicitation and definition process is entirely human-led; in the super-autonomous stage, the system can only act within the scope of existing review representations and cannot modify the definition of peace on its own. Advantages and Security: It addresses the ambiguity of the definition of "human peace," avoiding misunderstandings or conflicts arising from different cultural backgrounds. It maintains strict human-centered control: the final review authority rests with the system creator. It provides clear and computable ethical anchors for distributed nodes in the hyper-autonomous stage, ensuring that the process of cross-domain knowledge exchange to generate new knowledge always takes place within safe boundaries.

    In the context of large filters, this mechanism enables hyperautonomous meta-AI to possess powerful integration capabilities when facing existential crises, while also preventing it from deviating from fundamental human interests due to ambiguous definitions.

    User Mode: Continuously Learning Operational AI (For Users with No Prior Experience)

    User Mode: Meta-AI is AI that dynamically adapts to the user, is personalized AI, empowers the user, and is trustworthy AI.

    The core requirement is that we do not want AI capabilities to remain at the moment training is completed, but rather to be adaptive to users, personalized, and continuously learning, like Windows applications (different models are responsible for different services, ultimately serving the overall purpose). Trends in 2026:

    **Frozen Model → Adaptive Intelligence: The mainstream is shifting towards Continuous Learning + Test-Time Training (TTT). The model can "learn on the go" (without destroying existing knowledge) during inference, incorporating new information in real time through short-term memory modules (similar to Google's Titans architecture).

    Personalized Integration: The interface automatically adjusts based on user habits (e.g., if you frequently use image tasks, the vision module will be prioritized; if you prefer voice, the voice pipeline will be loaded first).
